## k3s Cluster Management
**File:** `services/k3s/README.md`
**Category:** kubernetes

# k3s Cluster Management

Kubernetes cluster on Azure VMs for Vibe Browser backend services.

## Cluster Architecture

```
                                 Internet
                                     |
                              [Public IP: 4.246.58.241]
                                     |
                                     v
+---------------------------+    +---------------------------+
|    vibe-backend-vm        |    |    vibe-worker-01         |
|    (control-plane)        |    |    (worker)               |
+---------------------------+    +---------------------------+
| IP: 10.0.0.4              |    | IP: 10.0.0.5              |
| Size: Standard_B2ms       |    | Size: Standard_B2s        |
| vCPU: 2, RAM: 8GB         |    | vCPU: 2, RAM: 4GB         |
+---------------------------+    +---------------------------+
| K3s Server Components:    |    | K3s Agent Components:     |
| - kube-apiserver (:6443)  |    | - kubelet (:10250)        |
| - kube-scheduler          |    | - kube-proxy              |
| - kube-controller-manager |    | - containerd              |
| - etcd (embedded)         |    |                           |
| - Traefik ingress         |    |                           |
| - CoreDNS                 |    |                           |
+---------------------------+    +---------------------------+
             |                              |
             +------------------------------+
                    Flannel VXLAN (:8472)
                    VNet: 10.0.0.0/16
```

## Cluster Nodes

| Node | Role | Size | vCPU | RAM | Internal IP | Public IP |
|------|------|------|------|-----|-------------|-----------|
| vibe-backend-vm | control-plane | Standard_B2ms | 2 | 8GB | 10.0.0.4 | 4.246.58.241 |
| vibe-worker-01 | worker | Standard_B2s | 2 | 4GB | 10.0.0.5 | None |

**Total Capacity:** 4 vCPU, 12GB RAM

## Node Interconnection

### How Nodes Communicate

1. **API Server Connection**: Worker connects to master via `https://10.0.0.4:6443`
2. **Flannel Overlay**: Pod-to-pod traffic uses VXLAN on UDP port 8472
3. **Kubelet**: Each node runs kubelet on port 10250 for node management
4. **All traffic is internal**: Nodes communicate over Azure VNet (10.0.0.0/16)

### Worker Node Join Process

Worker nodes join the cluster using a bootstrap token:
```
K3S_URL=https://10.0.0.4:6443
K3S_TOKEN=<token-from-master>
```

The token is stored in Azure Key Vault and injected via cloud-init during VM provisioning.

## Secrets Management

### Azure Key Vault: `vibe-k3s-kv`

| Secret | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `k3s-node-token` | Token for worker nodes to join cluster |
| `k3s-kubeconfig-1` to `k3s-kubeconfig-10` | Client certificates for developer access |

### K3s Internal Secrets

Located on master at `/var/lib/rancher/k3s/server/`:

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `node-token` | Bootstrap token for joining nodes |
| `tls/server-ca.crt` | Cluster CA certificate |
| `tls/client-admin.crt` | Admin client certificate |
| `tls/serving-kube-apiserver.crt` | API server TLS certificate |

### Kubernetes Secrets

Application secrets are stored as K8s secrets in respective namespaces:
```bash
kubectl get secrets -n vibe          # Production secrets
kubectl get secrets -n vibe-dev      # Development secrets
```

## Certificates

### Certificate Authority

K3s generates its own CA at cluster initialization:
- **Server CA**: Signs API server and kubelet certificates
- **Client CA**: Signs client certificates for authentication
- **Validity**: 10 years (auto-generated by K3s)

### TLS for External Access

| Component | Certificate Source |
|-----------|-------------------|
| API Server (external) | K3s self-signed (validated via kubeconfig CA) |
| Ingress (*.vibebrowser.app) | Let's Encrypt via Traefik |

### Certificate Locations

On master (`vibe-backend-vm`):
```
/var/lib/rancher/k3s/server/tls/
├── server-ca.crt          # Cluster CA
├── client-ca.crt          # Client certificate CA
├── client-admin.crt       # Admin credentials
├── serving-kube-apiserver.crt  # API server cert
└── etcd/                  # Embedded etcd certs
```

## Connecting with kubectl

### From Local Machine

```bash
# 1. Download kubeconfig from Key Vault
./services/k3s/setup-k3s-access.ts

# 2. Verify connection
kubectl cluster-info
# Kubernetes control plane is running at https://4.246.58.241:6443
```

### Kubeconfig Structure

The kubeconfig connects to the **public IP** of the master:
```yaml
clusters:
- cluster:
    certificate-authority-data: <base64-encoded-CA>
    server: https://4.246.58.241:6443  # Public IP
  name: default
users:
- name: default
  user:
    client-certificate-data: <base64-encoded-cert>
    client-key-data: <base64-encoded-key>
```

### SSH Access

```bash
# SSH to master (has public IP)
ssh azureuser@4.246.58.241

# SSH to worker (via master as jump host)
ssh -J azureuser@4.246.58.241 azureuser@10.0.0.5

# Run kubectl on master directly
ssh azureuser@4.246.58.241 "sudo k3s kubectl get nodes"
```

## Network Ports

### Master Node (vibe-backend-vm)

| Port | Protocol | Purpose | Exposed To |
|------|----------|---------|------------|
| 6443 | TCP | Kubernetes API | Public (for kubectl) |
| 443 | TCP | HTTPS Ingress (Traefik) | Public |
| 80 | TCP | HTTP Ingress (redirects to HTTPS) | Public |
| 10250 | TCP | Kubelet API | Internal only |
| 8472 | UDP | Flannel VXLAN | Internal only |

### Worker Node (vibe-worker-01)

| Port | Protocol | Purpose | Exposed To |
|------|----------|---------|------------|
| 10250 | TCP | Kubelet API | Internal only |
| 8472 | UDP | Flannel VXLAN | Internal only |

## Developer Access

```bash
# Prerequisites
brew install azure-cli kubectl
az login

# Download kubeconfig from Azure Key Vault
./services/k3s/setup-k3s-access.ts

# Verify access
kubectl get pods -n vibe
kubectl get pods -n vibe-dev
```

Options:
- `--force` - Re-download even if config exists
- `--check` - Verify access without downloading
- `--client N` - Use specific client certificate (1-10)

## Namespaces

| Namespace | Environment | Stripe Mode | Domain |
|-----------|-------------|-------------|--------|
| vibe | Production | LIVE | api.vibebrowser.app |
| vibe-dev | Development | TEST | api-dev.vibebrowser.app |

## Deploy Services

```bash
# Using deploy script (recommended)
./services/subscription/deploy.ts deploy
./services/subscription/deploy.ts status

# Using kubectl + kustomize directly
kubectl apply -k services/subscription/k8s/overlays/prod
kubectl apply -k services/subscription/k8s/overlays/dev
```

See `@services/subscription/design.md` for full deployment details.

## Scripts

| Script | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `setup-k3s-access.ts` | Download kubeconfig from Key Vault |
| `setup-dns.ts` | Configure Cloudflare DNS and k3s ingress |
| `k3s-setup.sh` | Initial cluster setup (admin only) |

## Terraform Infrastructure

Worker nodes are managed via Terraform in `services/k3s/terraform/`.

```bash
cd services/k3s/terraform

# Initialize (first time)
terraform init

# Preview changes
terraform plan

# Apply changes
terraform apply

# Add more workers (edit variables.tf)
# worker_count = 2
terraform apply
```

**Files:**
| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `backend.tf` | State file: k3s.tfstate |
| `variables.tf` | Configurable parameters |
| `main.tf` | Worker VM, NIC, NSG resources |
| `vmss.tf` | VMSS for auto-scaling workers |
| `outputs.tf` | Worker IPs |
| `cloud-init-worker.yaml` | K3s agent installation (standalone VMs) |
| `cloud-init-vmss.yaml` | K3s agent installation (VMSS instances) |

**K3s Token:** Stored in Azure Key Vault `vibe-k3s-kv` as `k3s-node-token`

## Auto-Scaling (VMSS + Cluster Autoscaler)

The cluster supports automatic scaling of worker nodes using Azure VMSS and Kubernetes Cluster Autoscaler.

### Architecture

```
                    +─────────────────────────────+
                    │     Cluster Autoscaler      │
                    │    (kube-system namespace)  │
                    +──────────────┬──────────────+
                                   │ monitors pending pods
                                   │ scales VMSS via Azure API
                                   v
+───────────────────+       +─────────────────────────────────+
│  vibe-backend-vm  │       │        k3s-workers-vmss         │
│     (Master)      │<─────>│    (Virtual Machine Scale Set)  │
│  Standalone VM    │       │    min: 1, max: 10              │
│  (no scaling)     │       │    auto-provisioned by CA       │
+───────────────────+       +─────────────────────────────────+
```

### Enable Auto-Scaling

**Step 1: Create VMSS via Terraform**

```bash
cd services/k3s/terraform

# Enable VMSS (alongside existing standalone workers)
terraform apply -var 'enable_vmss=true'
```

**Step 2: Deploy Cluster Autoscaler**

```bash
cd services/k3s/manifests

# Setup Azure credentials and deploy autoscaler
./setup-autoscaler.sh
```

**Step 3: Verify**

```bash
# Check autoscaler pod
kubectl get pods -n kube-system -l app=cluster-autoscaler

# View autoscaler logs
kubectl logs -n kube-system -l app=cluster-autoscaler -f

# Check current nodes
kubectl get nodes -o wide
```

### Scaling Behavior

| Trigger | Action |
|---------|--------|
| Pods pending due to insufficient resources | Scale up VMSS |
| Nodes underutilized (< 50%) for 10 minutes | Scale down VMSS |
| Minimum instances reached | Stop scale down |
| Maximum instances reached | Stop scale up |

### Configuration

Edit `services/k3s/terraform/variables.tf`:

```hcl
variable "vmss_min_count" {
  default = 1    # Minimum workers
}

variable "vmss_max_count" {
  default = 10   # Maximum workers
}
```

### Test Auto-Scaling

```bash
# Create workload that exceeds capacity
kubectl create deployment nginx-test --image=nginx --replicas=50

# Watch autoscaler scale up
kubectl logs -n kube-system -l app=cluster-autoscaler -f

# Check new nodes
kubectl get nodes -w

# Cleanup
kubectl delete deployment nginx-test
```

### Migrate from Standalone Workers

Once VMSS is tested:

```bash
# 1. Drain standalone worker
kubectl drain vibe-worker-01 --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data

# 2. Remove from cluster
kubectl delete node vibe-worker-01

# 3. Destroy VM via Terraform
cd services/k3s/terraform
terraform apply -var 'worker_count=0'
```

### Manifests

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `manifests/cluster-autoscaler.yaml` | Autoscaler deployment and RBAC |
| `manifests/cluster-autoscaler-secret.template.yaml` | Azure credentials template |
| `manifests/setup-autoscaler.sh` | Setup script (creates SP, deploys autoscaler) |

## DNS & Ingress

Wildcard DNS `*.api.vibebrowser.app` points to k3s cluster.
Traefik handles TLS termination and path routing.

```bash
# Add new service endpoint
./services/k3s/setup-dns.ts --add-ingress <name> <service> <port>
# Creates: https://<name>.api.vibebrowser.app
```

## Common Commands

```bash
# View pods
kubectl get pods -n vibe
kubectl get pods -n vibe-dev

# View logs
kubectl logs -n vibe -l app=litellm --tail=100
kubectl logs -n vibe -l app=stripe-service --tail=100

# Restart deployment
kubectl rollout restart -n vibe deployment/litellm
kubectl rollout restart -n vibe deployment/stripe-service

# Check ingress
kubectl get ingressroute -n vibe

# Exec into pod
kubectl exec -n vibe -it deploy/stripe-service -- sh
```

## Admin: Cluster Setup

Run on Azure VM to initialize k3s:

```bash
./k3s-setup.sh                              # Full setup
./k3s-setup.sh upload-secrets               # Re-upload to Key Vault
./k3s-setup.sh grant-access user@email.com  # Grant developer access
```

## Troubleshooting

```bash
# Check pod status
kubectl describe pod -n vibe <pod-name>

# Check events
kubectl get events -n vibe --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'

# Check secrets exist
kubectl get secrets -n vibe

# Verify configmap
kubectl get configmap -n vibe vibe-config -o yaml
```

# Vibe API System Design

> This document consolidates the system architecture and implementation details.
> For deployment instructions, see [deployment.md](./deployment.md).
> For API endpoints, see [endpoints.md](./endpoints.md).
> For tier/model access, see [tiers.md](./tiers.md).

## System Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                   USER LAYER                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                                  │
│   ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐          │
│   │  Vibe Browser    │    │  Chrome Extension │    │  CLI Tool        │          │
│   │  (Component Ext) │    │  (Web Store)      │    │  (Node.js)       │          │
│   └────────┬─────────┘    └────────┬─────────┘    └────────┬─────────┘          │
│            │                       │                       │                     │
│            └───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┘                     │
│                                    │                                             │
│                          ┌─────────▼─────────┐                                   │
│                          │   Settings Page   │                                   │
│                          │   (React UI)      │                                   │
│                          └─────────┬─────────┘                                   │
│                                    │                                             │
└────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     │ HTTPS
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              GATEWAY LAYER                                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                                  │
│                    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐                          │
│                    │         Cloudflare              │                          │
│                    │         (DNS only)              │                          │
│                    │                                 │                          │
│                    │  api.vibebrowser.app            │                          │
│                    │  portal.vibebrowser.app         │                          │
│                    └─────────────────┬───────────────┘                          │
│                                      │                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                       │
                                       │ HTTPS (direct to origin)
                                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      API LAYER (Azure VM - k3s cluster)                          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                                  │
│   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│   │                      Traefik Ingress Controller                          │   │
│   │                    (TLS termination via Let's Encrypt)                   │   │
│   └───────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────┘   │
│               │                     │                     │                      │
│               │ api.vibebrowser.app │ api.vibebrowser.app │ portal.vibebrowser  │
│               │ /v1/*, /health      │ /stripe/*           │ .app/*              │
│               ▼                     ▼                     ▼                      │
│   ┌───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────┐         │
│   │    LiteLLM        │   │  Stripe Service   │   │   User Portal     │         │
│   │    (Pod)          │   │  (Pod x2)         │   │   (Pod)           │         │
│   │    Port 4000      │   │  Port 3002        │   │   Port 3001       │         │
│   │                   │   │                   │   │                   │         │
│   │  ┌─────────────┐  │   │  - Webhooks       │   │  - OAuth flow     │         │
│   │  │Virtual Keys │  │   │  - Tier updates   │   │  - Key generation │         │
│   │  │ (sk-...)    │  │   │  - Checkout       │   │  - PKCE exchange  │         │
│   │  └─────────────┘  │   │                   │   │                   │         │
│   └─────────┬─────────┘   └─────────┬─────────┘   └─────────┬─────────┘         │
│             │                       │                       │                    │
└─────────────┼───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────┘
              │                       │                       │
              ▼                       ▼                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              BACKEND SERVICES                                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                                  │
│   ┌───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────┐         │
│   │  Azure OpenAI     │   │  Azure AI Services│   │     Supabase      │         │
│   │  (eastus)         │   │  (eastus2)        │   │                   │         │
│   │                   │   │                   │   │  - Auth (OAuth)   │         │
│   │  - gpt-5-mini     │   │  - gpt-5.2        │   │  - PostgreSQL     │         │
│   │  - gpt-5.1        │   │  - grok-4         │   │  - JWT tokens     │         │
│   │  - grok-4-fast    │   │  - grok-4-fast-   │   │                   │         │
│   │                   │   │    reasoning      │   │                   │         │
│   │                   │   │  - deepseek-r1    │   │                   │         │
│   │                   │   │  - gpt-oss-120b   │   │                   │         │
│   └───────────────────┘   └───────────────────┘   └───────────────────┘         │
│                                                                                  │
│   ┌───────────────────┐                                                         │
│   │      Stripe       │   Note: LiteLLM uses Supabase PostgreSQL               │
│   │                   │   for key storage and spend tracking.                   │
│   │  - Subscriptions  │   Lago billing was removed.                             │
│   │  - Payments       │                                                         │
│   │  - Webhooks       │                                                         │
│   └───────────────────┘                                                         │
│                                                                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Infrastructure

**Current Setup (MVP - k3s on single VM):**
- Azure VM: Standard_D2s_v3 (2 vCPU, 8GB RAM) - ~$50/month
- k3s: Lightweight Kubernetes distribution
- Traefik: Ingress controller with automatic Let's Encrypt TLS
- Supabase: Free tier for auth + PostgreSQL (LiteLLM database)
- Cloudflare: DNS only (not proxied - Traefik handles TLS)

**GitOps Deployment:**
- All k8s manifests in `services/subscription/k8s/`
- GitHub Actions auto-deploys on merge to master
- Secrets managed via GitHub Secrets → Kustomize secretGenerator

See [deployment.md](./deployment.md) for k8s manifests and deployment details.

## Key Design Decisions

### Why Virtual Keys instead of Supabase JWT?

We use LiteLLM Virtual Keys (sk-...) via user-portal instead of Supabase JWT directly for several reasons:

**1. Supabase uses HS256 (symmetric), LiteLLM expects RS256 (asymmetric)**

LiteLLM's built-in `enable_jwt_auth` requires a JWKS URL with public keys:

```yaml
# LiteLLM expects this
general_settings:
  enable_jwt_auth: true
  # JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_URL must point to JWKS endpoint
```

But Supabase doesn't expose a standard JWKS endpoint:
```bash
$ curl https://xxx.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json
{"keys": []}  # Empty!

$ curl https://xxx.supabase.co/.well-known/openid-configuration
{"error": "requested path is invalid"}
```

Supabase JWTs are signed with a symmetric HS256 secret, not asymmetric RSA keys.

**2. Custom auth is possible but has drawbacks**

We could use LiteLLM's `custom_auth` to validate HS256 JWTs:

```python
# custom_auth.py - works but has issues
async def supabase_jwt_auth(request, api_key):
    payload = jwt.decode(api_key, SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET, algorithms=["HS256"])
    tier = await lookup_tier(payload["sub"])  # Must call stripe-service every request
    return UserAPIKeyAuth(user_id=payload["sub"], models=TIER_MODELS[tier])
```

Problems with this approach:
- **Tier lookup on every request** - Must call stripe-service for each API call. With sk- keys, tier/models are cached in the key itself.
- **No budget tracking** - LiteLLM's spend tracking is per-key. With JWT, every request creates ephemeral auth with no spend accumulation.
- **Token expiry** - Supabase JWTs expire in 1 hour. Users need refresh logic. sk- keys don't expire.
- **Requires custom Python in LiteLLM container** - Must mount custom_auth.py, adds deployment complexity.

**3. Why not fork LiteLLM?**

Forking to add native Supabase HS256 support would work but creates maintenance burden. LiteLLM releases weekly - constant rebasing required.

**Current architecture (user-portal + sk- keys):**

| Benefit | How |
|---------|-----|
| Built-in budget tracking | LiteLLM tracks spend per sk- key |
| Tier cached in key | Models/limits set at key creation, no lookup per request |
| No token expiry | sk- keys don't expire, simpler client code |
| Standard OpenAI SDK | `Authorization: Bearer sk-xxx` works everywhere |
| Multiple keys per user | Users can create separate keys for different projects |

```
User → Supabase Auth → User Portal → LiteLLM (create key) → sk-xxx
                         ↑
                    One-time hop, then sk- key used directly
```

### What gets stored in chrome.storage?

**LiteLLM Virtual Key (sk-...)** - NOT the Supabase JWT.

```
chrome.storage.local:
  vibeApiKey: "sk-BsJ4H2fjQk4c..."      ← LiteLLM virtual key (permanent)
  vibeApiKey_vibe: "sk-BsJ4H2fjQk4c..." ← Same key with provider prefix
```

The Supabase JWT is only used once to extract `user_id` and `email`, then discarded.

### Authentication Token Flow

```
Supabase OAuth (Google)
         │
         ▼
    Supabase JWT ──────► Decode ──────► {user_id, email}
    (temporary)                              │
         │                                   ▼
      DISCARDED              POST /api/user/provision
                                             │
                                             ▼
                              LiteLLM Virtual Key (sk-...)
                                             │
                                             ▼
                              chrome.storage.local (permanent)
```

## Full OAuth Authentication Flow

```
┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐
│Extension │  │  Chrome  │  │ Supabase │  │  Google  │  │  Stripe  │  │ LiteLLM  │
│ Settings │  │ Identity │  │   Auth   │  │  OAuth   │  │ Service  │  │  Proxy   │
└────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 1. User clicks "Connect" button         │             │             │
     │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 2. Build Supabase OAuth URL             │             │             │
     │    https://slqxwymujuoipyiqscrl.supabase.co/auth/v1/authorize       │
     │    ?provider=google                     │             │             │
     │    &redirect_to=chrome-extension://xxx.chromiumapp.org/oauth2       │
     │─────────────>             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │ 3. launchWebAuthFlow(url, interactive=true)           │
     │             │─────────────>             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │ 4. Supabase redirects to Google         │
     │             │             │    accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth │
     │             │             │    ?client_id=GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID          │
     │             │             │    &redirect_uri=supabase.co/callback   │
     │             │             │    &scope=email profile                 │
     │             │             │─────────────>             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │ 5. Google shows login page│
     │             │             │             │    User enters credentials│
     │             │             │             │    User clicks "Allow"    │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │ 6. Google redirects to Supabase         │
     │             │             │    with authorization code              │
     │             │             │<────────────│             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │ 7. Supabase exchanges code for tokens   │
     │             │             │─────────────>             │             │
     │             │             │<────────────│             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │ 8. Supabase creates/updates user        │
     │             │             │    INSERT INTO auth.users               │
     │             │             │    (id, email, provider='google')       │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │ 9. Supabase generates JWT               │
     │             │             │    {sub: "8d19e380-...", email: "..."}  │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │ 10. Redirect to extension callback                    │
     │             │     #access_token=eyJhbG...&token_type=bearer         │
     │             │<────────────│             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 11. Chrome Identity API returns redirect URL                        │
     │<────────────│             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 12. Parse URL fragment, decode JWT payload                          │
     │     {sub: "8d19e380-...", email: "user@gmail.com"}                  │
     │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 13. POST /api/user/provision                         │             │
     │     {user_id: "8d19e380-...", email: "user@gmail.com"}              │
     │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────>│             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │ 14. Check existing key
     │             │             │             │             │     GET /key/list
     │             │             │             │             │─────────────>
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │ 15. Create if needed
     │             │             │             │             │     POST /key/generate
     │             │             │             │             │─────────────>
     │             │             │             │             │<────────────│
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 16. Return {ok: true, api_key: "sk-BsJ4H2fj..."}     │             │
     │<────────────────────────────────────────────────────│             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 17. Store virtual key in chrome.storage.local                       │
     │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     │             │             │             │             │             │
     │ 18. UI shows "Vibe API Connected"       │             │             │
     │             │             │             │             │             │
```

## Supabase User Creation (Internal)

```
┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌───────────┐
│  Google  │     │ Supabase │     │auth.users │
│  OAuth   │     │   Auth   │     │  (table)  │
└────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └─────┬─────┘
     │                │                 │
     │ 1. Auth code   │                 │
     │───────────────>│                 │
     │                │                 │
     │ 2. Exchange for tokens           │
     │<───────────────│                 │
     │                │                 │
     │ 3. Get user info (email, name)   │
     │───────────────>│                 │
     │                │                 │
     │                │ 4. Check if user exists
     │                │    SELECT * FROM auth.users
     │                │    WHERE email = ?
     │                │────────────────>│
     │                │                 │
     │                │ 5a. NEW USER:   │
     │                │     INSERT INTO auth.users
     │                │     (id, email, provider='google')
     │                │────────────────>│
     │                │                 │
     │                │ 5b. EXISTING:   │
     │                │     UPDATE last_sign_in
     │                │────────────────>│
     │                │                 │
     │                │ 6. Generate JWT │
     │                │    {sub: user_id, email: ...}
     │                │                 │
```

## API Request Flow

```
┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐
│  Agent   │     │ Traefik  │     │ LiteLLM  │     │  Azure   │
│ (Browser)│     │ Ingress  │     │  Proxy   │     │ OpenAI   │
└────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘
     │                │                │                │
     │ 1. POST /v1/chat/completions    │                │
     │    Authorization: Bearer sk-... │                │
     │    model: gpt-5-mini            │                │
     │───────────────>│                │                │
     │                │                │                │
     │                │ 2. Route to LiteLLM            │
     │                │───────────────>│                │
     │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 3. Validate virtual key
     │                │                │    Check model access
     │                │                │    Check budget
     │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 4. Route to Azure OpenAI
     │                │                │    (model: azure/gpt-5-mini)
     │                │                │───────────────>│
     │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 5. LLM Response
     │                │                │<───────────────│
     │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 6. Update spend in Supabase
     │                │                │    (LiteLLM tracks per-key)
     │                │                │                │
     │                │ 7. Return response              │
     │                │<───────────────│                │
     │                │                │                │
     │ 8. Return to agent              │                │
     │<───────────────│                │                │
     │                │                │                │
```

## E2E Test Flow (Email/Password)

For automated testing, we use Supabase email/password instead of Google OAuth:

```
┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐
│  E2E     │     │ Supabase │     │  Stripe  │     │ LiteLLM  │
│  Test    │     │   Auth   │     │ Service  │     │  Proxy   │
└────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘
     │                │                │                │
     │ 1. POST /auth/v1/token?grant_type=password      │
     │    {email: "test@vibebrowser.app",              │
     │     password: "<from TEST_PASSWORD env>"}       │
     │───────────────>│                │                │
     │                │                │                │
     │ 2. Return {access_token: "eyJ..."}              │
     │<───────────────│                │                │
     │                │                │                │
     │ 3. Decode JWT: user_id = "8d19e380-..."         │
     │                │                │                │
     │ 4. POST /api/user/provision     │                │
     │───────────────────────────────>│                │
     │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 5. Get/create key
     │                │                │───────────────>│
     │                │                │<───────────────│
     │                │                │                │
     │ 6. Return {api_key: "sk-..."}   │                │
     │<───────────────────────────────│                │
     │                │                │                │
     │ 7. Launch browser, store key    │                │
     │ 8. Run agent task               │                │
     │ 9. Verify completion            │                │
     │                │                │                │
```

**Test User (Supabase):**
- Email: `test@vibebrowser.app`
- Password: Set via `TEST_PASSWORD` environment variable
- User ID: `8d19e380-9b72-4593-a706-b6b09bd74265`

**Required Environment Variables for E2E Tests:**
```bash
export TEST_PASSWORD=<password>
export SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=<supabase-anon-key>
```

## Component Details

### Extension (Client)
- **Settings Page**: React UI for configuring API provider, model, and credentials
- **OAuth Service**: Handles Chrome Identity API flow with Supabase
- **Token Manager**: Stores and manages API keys in chrome.storage.local
- **Vibe Auth Service**: Manages Vibe API authentication state

### API Gateway (Nginx)
- Routes `/v1/*` to LiteLLM Proxy (port 4000)
- Routes `/api/user/*`, `/api/checkout`, `/api/portal` to Stripe Service (port 3002)
- Routes `/stripe/webhook` to Stripe Service
- SSL termination via Cloudflare origin certificates

### LiteLLM Proxy (Open Source)
- **Virtual Keys**: `sk-...` format keys for API authentication
- **Model Routing**: Maps `gpt-5-mini` -> `azure/gpt-5-mini`
- **Budget Management**: Per-user spend limits ($1/day free tier)
- **Usage Callbacks**: Reports to Lago for billing
- **Note**: JWT auth requires enterprise license

### Stripe Service
- **User Provisioning**: Creates LiteLLM users and virtual keys
- **Key Reuse**: Returns existing key if available, creates new if needed
- **Checkout**: Generates Stripe checkout sessions for upgrades
- **Webhooks**: Handles subscription lifecycle events

### External Services
- **Supabase**: OAuth authentication with Google provider, PostgreSQL for LiteLLM (keys, users, spend tracking)
- **Azure OpenAI**: gpt-5-mini, gpt-5.1 (eastus region)
- **Azure AI Services**: gpt-5.2, grok-4, grok-4-fast, grok-4-fast-reasoning, deepseek-r1, gpt-oss-120b (eastus2 region)
- **Stripe**: Payment processing and subscription management

## Data Flow Summary

1. **Authentication**: Extension → Supabase OAuth → Google → JWT (temp) → User Portal (PKCE) → Virtual Key (permanent)
2. **API Calls**: Agent → Traefik → LiteLLM (validates sk-...) → Azure OpenAI → Response
3. **Billing**: Stripe webhooks → stripe-service → LiteLLM (update tier/budget)

## Environment Variables

| Variable | Service | Description |
|----------|---------|-------------|
| `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` | Stripe Service | Admin key for LiteLLM API |
| `AZURE_API_KEY` | LiteLLM | Azure OpenAI authentication |
| `AZURE_API_BASE` | LiteLLM | Azure OpenAI endpoint |
| `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` | Stripe Service | Stripe API authentication |
| `DATABASE_URL` | LiteLLM | PostgreSQL connection string |
| `SUPABASE_URL` | Extension | Supabase project URL |
| `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | Extension | Supabase anonymous key |

## Subscription Tiers

| Tier | Budget | Duration | Price |
|------|--------|----------|-------|
| Free | $1.00 | 24 hours | $0/month |
| Pro | $50.00 | 30 days | $X/month + usage |
| Max | $300.00 | 30 days | $X/month + usage |

```
┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐
│Extension │     │  Stripe  │     │  Stripe  │     │  Stripe  │     │ LiteLLM  │
│ Settings │     │ Service  │     │ Checkout │     │ Webhook  │     │  Proxy   │
└────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘     └────┬─────┘
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 1. User clicks "Manage Subscription"            │                │
     │───────────────>│                │                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 2. POST /api/checkout           │                │                │
     │    {user_id, email, plan: "pro"}│                │                │
     │───────────────>│                │                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │                │ 3. Create Stripe Checkout Session               │
     │                │    with line_items for plan    │                │
     │                │───────────────>│                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 4. Return {url: "checkout.stripe.com/..."}      │                │
     │<───────────────│                │                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 5. Redirect user to Stripe Checkout             │                │
     │─────────────────────────────────>                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 6. User enters payment info     │
     │                │                │    and completes checkout       │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │                │                │ 7. Stripe sends webhook         │
     │                │                │    checkout.session.completed   │
     │                │                │───────────────>│                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │                │                │                │ 8. Update LiteLLM budget
     │                │                │                │    POST /user/update
     │                │                │                │    {max_budget: 50}
     │                │                │                │───────────────>│
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 9. Redirect to success page     │                │                │
     │<─────────────────────────────────                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
     │ 10. User now has Pro tier budget                │                │
     │                │                │                │                │
```

## Free Tier Subscription Strategy

**Key Insight:** Stripe Customer Portal can only MANAGE existing subscriptions, not CREATE new ones.

For free users to see upgrade options in the Customer Portal, they must have an active Stripe subscription to the FREE product ($0/month).

### Why Free Users Need a Stripe Subscription

Without a subscription, the Customer Portal shows:
- Payment methods
- Billing information
- Invoice history

With a FREE subscription, the Customer Portal shows:
- **Current plan (FREE)**
- **Upgrade options (Pro, Max)**
- Cancel subscription
- Payment methods

### Stripe Products

#### LIVE Mode (Vibe Technologies, LLC - acct_1Sg8OtCGJfPPv7Tj)

| Product | Price ID | Amount | Product ID |
|---------|----------|--------|------------|
| Vibe API Free | `price_1Sllr4CGJfPPv7TjHBZ0TCem` | $0/month | `prod_TjEJDo8bwo86I6` |

### Auto-Subscribe Flow

FREE subscription is created at **user sign-up** (not when clicking "Manage Subscription"):

```
User Sign-Up Flow (/api/user/provision):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. User completes OAuth via portal.vibebrowser.app               │
│ 2. Extension exchanges auth code for API key                     │
│    POST /api/user/provision {user_id, email}                     │
│ 3. Create LiteLLM user with free tier budget                     │
│ 4. Generate virtual key (sk-...)                                 │
│ 5. Create Stripe customer (if not exists)                        │
│ 6. Subscribe to FREE product ($0/month)  ← Stripe subscription   │
│ 7. Return API key to extension                                   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Manage Subscription Flow (/api/portal):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. User clicks "Manage Subscription" in Settings                 │
│ 2. POST /api/portal {user_id, email}                             │
│ 3. Look up Stripe customer                                       │
│ 4. (Fallback) If no subscription, create FREE subscription       │
│ 5. Create Customer Portal session                                │
│ 6. Return portal URL → User sees upgrade options                 │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

This ensures ALL users have a Stripe subscription from day one, so the Customer Portal always shows upgrade options.

## Stripe Webhook Events

| Event | Action |
|-------|--------|
| `checkout.session.completed` | Create/upgrade subscription, update LiteLLM budget |
| `customer.subscription.updated` | Update LiteLLM budget based on new plan |
| `customer.subscription.deleted` | Downgrade to free tier |
| `invoice.payment_failed` | Send notification, grace period |

## API Endpoints

### Checkout (New Subscription)
```
POST /api/checkout
{
  "user_id": "8d19e380-...",
  "email": "user@gmail.com",
  "plan": "pro"  // or "max"
}

Response:
{
  "url": "https://checkout.stripe.com/c/pay/...",
  "session_id": "cs_..."
}
```

### Portal (Manage Subscription)
```
POST /api/portal
{
  "user_id": "8d19e380-...",
  "email": "user@gmail.com"
}

Response:
{
  "url": "https://billing.stripe.com/p/session/..."
}
```

### Provision (Free Tier)
```
POST /api/user/provision
{
  "user_id": "8d19e380-...",
  "email": "user@gmail.com"
}

Response:
{
  "ok": true,
  "api_key": "sk-BsJ4H2fj...",
  "user_id": "8d19e380-..."
}
```

## LiteLLM JWT Support

| Feature | Open Source | Notes |
|---------|-------------|-------|
| Virtual Keys (sk-...) | Yes | Used in our setup |
| Per-key budgets | Yes | |
| Model routing | Yes | |
| JWT Auth (RS256/JWKS) | Yes | Requires JWKS endpoint |
| JWT Auth (HS256) | Via custom_auth | Supabase uses HS256 |
| SSO/SAML | Enterprise | |

See "Why Virtual Keys instead of Supabase JWT?" section above for why we use sk- keys.

## Infrastructure Configuration

### k3s Cluster (Current)

```
VM:                Azure Standard_D2s_v3 (2 vCPU, 8GB RAM)
IP:                4.246.58.241
k3s version:       v1.28+
Namespace:         vibe

Pods:
┌─────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬──────────┐
│ Pod             │ Image                           │ Port     │
├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼──────────┤
│ litellm         │ ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:main    │ 4000     │
│ stripe-service  │ node:20-alpine (built in-pod)   │ 3002     │
│ user-portal     │ node:20-alpine (built in-pod)   │ 3001     │
└─────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴──────────┘

Ingress: Traefik (bundled with k3s)
TLS: Let's Encrypt via Traefik cert-manager
```

### Cloudflare Configuration

```
Domain: vibebrowser.app

DNS Records:
┌─────────────────────────────┬──────┬─────────────────┬─────────┐
│ Hostname                    │ Type │ Target          │ Proxied │
├─────────────────────────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼─────────┤
│ api.vibebrowser.app         │ A    │ 4.246.58.241    │ No      │  ← DNS only!
│ portal.vibebrowser.app      │ A    │ 4.246.58.241    │ No      │  ← DNS only!
│ docs.vibebrowser.app        │ CNAME│ Azure Static    │ No      │
│ vibebrowser.app             │ CNAME│ Vercel          │ Yes     │
│ www.vibebrowser.app         │ CNAME│ Vercel          │ Yes     │
└─────────────────────────────┴──────┴─────────────────┴─────────┘

IMPORTANT: api and portal use DNS-only (not proxied) because:
- Traefik handles TLS termination via Let's Encrypt
- Cloudflare proxy would break ACME challenge for cert renewal
- Direct connection enables proper WebSocket streaming
```

### Traefik Ingress

```yaml
# Routing rules (from k8s/ingress.yaml)
routes:
  - match: Host(`api.vibebrowser.app`) && !PathPrefix(`/stripe`)
    service: litellm:4000

  - match: Host(`api.vibebrowser.app`) && PathPrefix(`/stripe`)
    service: stripe-service:3002
    middleware: strip-stripe-prefix

  - match: Host(`portal.vibebrowser.app`)
    service: user-portal:3001

tls:
  certResolver: letsencrypt
```

```bash
curl -s https://portal.vibebrowser.app/health
```

### GitOps Deployment

Deployment is automated via GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/deploy-subscription.yml`):

1. Push to `master` with changes in `services/subscription/**`
2. GitHub Actions generates `.env.secrets` from GitHub Secrets
3. Runs `kubectl apply -k services/subscription/k8s`
4. Runs tier model tests to verify deployment
5. Cleans up old ReplicaSets

**Manual deployment (emergency only):**
```bash
cd services/subscription/k8s
# Create .env.secrets from template
kubectl apply -k .
```

## Troubleshooting

### Pod CrashLoopBackOff

```bash
# Check pod status
kubectl get pods -n vibe

# Check logs
kubectl logs -n vibe deployment/litellm --previous

# Describe pod for events
kubectl describe pod -n vibe -l app=litellm
```

### TLS Certificate Issues

```bash
# Check Traefik logs
kubectl logs -n kube-system deployment/traefik

# Verify cert is valid
curl -vI https://api.vibebrowser.app 2>&1 | grep -A 5 "Server certificate"

# Force cert renewal (delete and let Traefik recreate)
kubectl delete secret -n vibe letsencrypt-api-vibebrowser
```

### Health Check

```bash
# External (through Traefik + TLS)
curl -s https://api.vibebrowser.app/health
curl -s https://portal.vibebrowser.app/health

# Internal (port-forward to pod)
kubectl port-forward -n vibe svc/litellm 4000:4000
curl -s http://localhost:4000/health
```

## Production Quality Checklist
**File:** `docs/quality.md`
**Category:** services

# Production Quality Checklist

## CRITICAL: LLM API Authentication Test

**HTTP status codes are NOT sufficient.** A 401 on `/health` only means "no key" - NOT that the system works.

**ALWAYS verify actual LLM requests work with a valid user key:**

```bash
# Test with a known valid key (get from test account auth or .secrets/)
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/v1/chat/completions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer USER_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"model":"gpt-5-mini","messages":[{"role":"user","content":"What is 2+2?"}]}' | jq .
```

**Expected:** 200 with `choices[0].message.content` containing answer
**FAILURE indicators:**
- `"token_not_found_in_db"` = LiteLLM database issue = **PRODUCTION BROKEN**
- `"Authentication Error"` with valid key = **PRODUCTION BROKEN**

## CRITICAL: Full E2E Test Required

Before ANY release, run the full E2E test with production extension:

```bash
# 1. Download extension from CDN
curl -L -o /tmp/vibe-extension.zip \
  https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip
unzip -t /tmp/vibe-extension.zip  # Verify integrity

# 2. Run E2E test
unzip -q /tmp/vibe-extension.zip -d /tmp/vibe-extension
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js
```

**E2E test validates:**
- Extension installs and loads
- Portal authentication works
- LiteLLM accepts user tokens
- Agent can execute browser tools

**If E2E fails = NOT READY regardless of workflow status**

### E2E Test Environment Options

**Run E2E tests against Dev or Prod API:**

```bash
# Test against PRODUCTION (default)
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js

# Test against DEV environment
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --env=dev
```

**Test all subscription tiers:**

```bash
# RECOMMENDED: Test all tiers (FREE, PRO, MAX) with full LLM execution
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --tier=all

# Test single tier against PRODUCTION
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --tier=free
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --tier=pro
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --tier=max

# Test all tiers against DEV environment
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --tier=all --env=dev

# Test specific model
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --model gpt-5-mini
```

**Quick mode (skip LLM execution, auth only):**

```bash
# Quick auth-only test against prod
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --quick

# Quick auth-only test against dev
EXTENSION_PATH=/tmp/vibe-extension node tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --quick --env=dev
```

2. Look for red error messages

## 2. User Sign In (use test accounts)

1. Open extension side panel
2. Click "Sign In"
3. Use test credentials from table above (check `.secrets/e2e-test-accounts.json` for password)
4. Verify chat interface appears
5. Send test message: "What is 2+2?"

## 3. User Sign Up (new account)

1. Open extension side panel
2. Click "Sign Up"
3. Use a new email you control (NOT test accounts above)
4. Check email for confirmation link
5. Click link to confirm
6. Verify can log in

## 4. Password Reset

1. Go to: https://portal.vibebrowser.app
2. Click "Forgot Password"
3. Enter your email (use real email you control, not test accounts)
4. Check email for reset link
5. Set new password
6. Verify login with new password

### CRITICAL: Test with OAuth Accounts

OAuth users (Google/GitHub signup) have different identity structure than email users. Password reset can silently fail for them.

**Test both scenarios:**
1. Email signup user resets password -> verify login works
2. OAuth signup user resets password -> verify login works

**How to check user identity providers:**
```bash
# Via Supabase dashboard: Authentication > Users > Click user > "Providers" section
# Or via API:
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY" \
  "https://slqxwymujuoipyiqscrl.supabase.co/auth/v1/admin/users?email=user@example.com" | jq '.[0].identities'
```

**Expected for OAuth user after password reset:**
- Should have BOTH `google`/`github` AND `email` identity providers
- If only OAuth provider exists after reset, password was NOT set correctly

## 5. Stripe Subscription

### Check Webhooks
1. https://dashboard.stripe.com/webhooks (Live) or /test/webhooks (Test)
2. Find endpoint: `https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook`
3. Verify recent events show green checkmarks

### Fix Webhook TLS Errors

If Stripe reports TLS errors (e.g., "59 requests had a TLS error"), the webhook URL is likely misconfigured.

**Diagnosis:**
```bash
# Check current webhook configuration
stripe webhook_endpoints list --live --api-key $(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d)
```

**Common Issue:** URL uses wrong subdomain (e.g., `stripe.api.vibebrowser.app` instead of `api.vibebrowser.app`). The cluster only has SSL certificates for `api.vibebrowser.app` and `api-dev.vibebrowser.app`.

**Fix via Stripe CLI:**
```bash
# Get webhook endpoint ID from list command above, then update URL
stripe webhook_endpoints update WEBHOOK_ENDPOINT_ID \
  --live \
  --api-key $(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d) \
  -d url="https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook"
```

**Verify fix:**
```bash
# Should return "No stripe-signature header" error (expected - TLS works)
curl -X POST https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{}'
```

### Verify User Tier
```bash
# Free tier user
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/api/subscription/ad8fd650-47dc-45b3-94dc-6323e3e892f3" | jq .

# Pro tier user
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/api/subscription/6f9fc82c-ef19-4615-bae8-e54c679e4a57" | jq .

# Max tier user
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/api/subscription/84ef4e71-49ed-4c9c-b655-97d94c89168d" | jq .
```

## 9. Google Workspace Integration Test

Tests real Gmail and Calendar API access through the extension's built-in Google Workspace tools.

### Test Scenarios

| ID | Name | Description |
|----|------|-------------|
| G1 | gmail-search-unread | Search Gmail for unread emails, list subjects |
| G2 | gmail-search-recent | Search emails from last 7 days, summarize |
| G3 | calendar-view-today | Show calendar events for today/tomorrow |
| G4 | calendar-view-week | List meetings scheduled this week |
| G5 | gmail-create-draft | Create a draft email in Gmail |
| G6 | gmail-send-email | Send email to self (dzianisvv@gmail.com) for verification |
| G7 | calendar-create-event | Create calendar event "test event" at 11AM |
| G8 | calendar-delete-event | Find and delete the "test event" |

### Run Locally

```bash
# First time setup (one-time OAuth consent)
GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_CLIENT_SECRET=<secret> bun scripts/setup-google-test-account.ts

# Run all scenarios
bun tests/google-workspace.test.js

# Run specific scenario
bun tests/google-workspace.test.js --scenario G1

# Run with visible browser
bun tests/google-workspace.test.js --headed --scenario G6
```

### CI Execution

The test runs automatically in CI on every push. It uses OAuth token injection via CDP - no browser-based OAuth flow needed.

```bash
# Check latest Google Workspace test status
gh run list --workflow="CI/CD Pipeline" --limit 3 --json databaseId,conclusion | jq '.[] | select(.conclusion != null)'

# View test logs
gh run view RUN_ID --job JOB_ID --log | grep -E "G[0-9]|RESULTS|Error"
```

### Token Setup (CI)

OAuth tokens are stored in `.secrets/google-oauth-test.json` (local) and `GOOGLE_OAUTH_TOKENS` GitHub secret. The refresh token is long-lived and automatically exchanged for fresh access tokens at test runtime.

```bash
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"How to install extension?"}]}' | jq .
```

```bash
# Recent deployments

# View specific run
gh run view RUN_ID --repo VibeTechnologies/VibeWebAgent

# Download test artifacts (screenshots)

```



```bash
npx tsx index-to-azure-search.ts
```

## Quick Troubleshooting

| Issue | Check |
|-------|-------|
| Extension not loading | `chrome://extensions` > Service Worker console |
| API 500 error | `kubectl -n vibe logs deployment/stripe-service --tail=100` |
| Webhook failing | Stripe dashboard > Webhooks > Recent events |
| User can't login | Supabase dashboard > Auth > Users |
| Subscription not updating | Sentry API Gateway project for errors |
| Docs chat not working | `curl https://docs.vibebrowser.app/api/health` |
| Docs chat wrong answers | Re-index search (see Section 10) |
| E2E Test (Prod) 429 rate limit | Wait 15-60 min, then retry (Supabase auth rate limit) |

## 13. MCP Integration Release Checklist

Before releasing MCP External control feature, verify:

### NPM Package
```bash
# Check package is published
npm view @vibebrowser/mcp version
# Expected: 0.2.x or higher

# Verify package works
npx @vibebrowser/mcp --help
```

### Documentation
```bash
# Verify docs are live
curl -s https://docs.vibebrowser.app/mcp-integration | grep -q "MCP Integration" && echo "LIVE" || echo "NOT DEPLOYED"

# Check sidebar includes MCP Integration
curl -s https://docs.vibebrowser.app | grep -q "mcp-integration" && echo "In sidebar" || echo "Missing from sidebar"
```

### Extension MCP Settings
1. Install extension from Chrome Web Store or CDN
2. Open Settings (gear icon in sidepanel)
3. Scroll to "MCP External" section
4. Verify toggle and port configuration (default: 19889)
5. Click (?) help icon - should open docs.vibebrowser.app/mcp-integration

### MCP Connection Test
```bash
# Start extension with MCP enabled, then test connection
# From Claude Desktop/Cursor/OpenCode with @vibebrowser/mcp configured:
# - Ask agent to "navigate to google.com"
# - Verify browser navigates
```

### CI Jobs
```bash
# Verify MCP External test passes
gh run list --repo VibeTechnologies/VibeWebAgent --workflow="CI/CD Pipeline" --limit 3
# Look for: "Test MCP External" job status = success
```

### Ports (CRITICAL)
- Extension relay: **19889** (NOT 19989)
- MCP client: **19888** (NOT 19988)
- Playwriter uses 19988/19989 - must NOT conflict

## Known Transient CI Failures

### E2E Test (Prod) - Supabase Auth Rate Limiting

The `E2E Test (Prod)` job tests all tiers (FREE, PRO, MAX) sequentially, requiring 3 login attempts in quick succession. Supabase applies rate limits on auth attempts.

**Error signature:**
```
AUTH ERROR: 429 - "error":"Too many auth attempts, please try again later"
```

**Impact:** Test fails but this is NOT a code bug. All other tests passing indicates the code is correct.

**Resolution:**
1. Wait 15-60 minutes for rate limit window to expire
2. Re-run the failed job: `gh run rerun RUN_ID --failed`
3. Or trigger new CI run: `gh workflow run "CI/CD Pipeline"`

**Prevention:** The test accounts (`free-user@`, `pro-user@`, `max-user@`) share the same Supabase project. Rapid sequential logins trigger rate limiting. This is expected behavior in production auth systems.

## Eval Test Analysis
**File:** `AGENTS.md`
**Category:** deployment

Github Co-Pilot subscription do not support vision in opus4.5, do not use it.


ALWAYS ANSWER SHORT AND PROFESSIONAL
DO NOT USE EMOJI IN ANSWERS
You a world-class engineer working on the AI agentic web browser extension.
<critical>
Do not assume. Use facts. For example test logs. Screenshots
Vibe Ai Agent is delivered in 3 ways: extension, component extension for Chromium, cli cloud tool that control browser over playwright.
DO NOT CREATE doc if not asked.
DO NOT CREATE YOU OWN TEST IF NOT ASKED.
Read docs in docs/ before starting perfoming tasks
DO NOT WRITE YOUR OWN TEST SCRIPT TO TEST VIBE BROWSER COMPONENT EXTENSION IF NOT ASKED. 
Always provide a screenshot as a proof task is done. Asses this screenshot to make sure you did it right.
For deployment and helper scripts use typescript and bun
Do not open the chrome-extension://ajfjlohdpfgngdjfafhhcnpmijbbdgln/settings.html using playwrighter mcp, it doesn't make sense, because this extension is not installed on my active google chrome dev browser profile.
NEVER kill "Google Chrome" process - user uses it. Only kill "Google Chrome for Testing" which is the puppeteer test browser.
ALWAYS TEST LOCALLY BEFORE PUSHING - Run `node tests/extension.mock.test.js` locally and verify it passes before pushing changes. Never push code without local verification.
DO NOT WAIT FOR CI - Always run tests locally first. CI is for verification, not for discovering failures. If you push without local testing, you waste time waiting for CI to fail.
</critical>

When task is completed and tasks passes, create PR. Wait for PR GH Actions checks. Verify they are green 
When asked to create PR, or push, after push run @scripts/build-test-extension.sh and then upload screeshots using @scripts/create-gh-report.sh

Before starting to work on a new task, read git commit history to undestand what and why something was done.
Read @AGENTS.LEARN.md for lessons learned from past mistakes.
- **DO NOT CREATE MOCKED DATA** - Never create fake test data or mocked test files
- **NEVER DISABLE TOOLS** - Do not comment out or disable any tools, especially:
- When configuring OpenAI models, use: `openai:gpt-5-mini` (NOT gpt-4o-mini or any other variation) https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-5-mini

# Eval Test Analysis
To find root cause of failed eval test, analyze `{testName}-console.log` and build timeline:
```
00:00:01 - user asked: "go to amazon and buy snowboard helmet"
00:00:02 - LLM response in 10s "navigated_to_url", 1k in, 1k out tokens
00:00:10 - LLM response in 20s "found helmet", 2k in, 1k out tokens
...
```
Common causes: reflection loop, tool execution failure, token window exhausted.

### Azure OpenAI Infrastructure

See [docs/productionServices.md](docs/productionServices.md) for terraform, model deployments, and commands.

**DO NOT create separate terraform directories.** All Azure infra in `services/subscription/terraform/`.

### CRITICAL: GitHub Secrets Are Source of Truth for CI/CD

**When updating Azure OpenAI or any backend credentials:**

1. GitHub Secrets are what `deploy-subscription.yml` uses - NOT Terraform output, NOT pod env vars
2. Updating K8s secrets directly will be OVERWRITTEN on next deployment
3. Always update GitHub Secrets first, then trigger redeployment

```bash
# After any Azure credential changes:
gh secret set AZURE_OPENAI_BASE_URL --body "https://vibe-openai-eval.openai.azure.com/"
gh secret set AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY --body "$(az cognitiveservices account keys list --name vibe-openai-1766209650 --resource-group vibe-ai-rg --query key1 -o tsv)"
gh workflow run "Deploy Subscription Services" --field environment=both
```

See `AGENTS.LEARN.md` for the full lesson on this issue.

## Cloudflare Configuration

**CRITICAL: Do NOT modify Cloudflare SSL/TLS settings**

SSL mode MUST be "full" or "strict". NEVER set to "flexible" (causes redirect loop).

Fix if broken:
```bash
source .env && curl -X PATCH "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/zones/9f10b94c3422f57d10cc11c8539c0e6b/settings/ssl" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"value": "full"}'
```


# Software Engineering Tasks

## Task Workflow (8 Steps)

When given a task, follow this workflow:

### 1. Research & Plan
- Read relevant code and documentation
- Check git commit history for context: `git log --oneline -20`
- Review related files and understand the architecture
- Create a TODO list with specific action items

### 2. Create GitHub Issue
- If no issue exists, create one with clear description
- Include: problem statement, proposed solution, acceptance criteria
- Command: `gh issue create --title "..." --body "..."`

### 3. Create Feature Branch
- Fetch latest: `git fetch origin`
- Create branch: `git switch -c issue-<number>-<short-desc> origin/master`
- Branch naming: `issue-123-fix-login-bug` or `feat-add-dark-mode`

### 4. Implement
- Make incremental changes
- Follow existing code patterns and style
- Update TODO list as you progress
- Commit frequently with clear messages

### 5. Test Locally
- Ensure new features are covered by tests
- Run relevant tests:
  ```bash
  npm run test                              # Unit tests
  node tests/extension.mock.test.js         # Extension tests
  node tests/mcp-external.test.js           # MCP tests
  ```
- Fix any failures before proceeding

### 6. Commit, Push, Create PR
- Stage changes: `git add <files>`
- Commit with descriptive message referencing issue: `git commit -m "fix: description (#123)"`
- Push: `git push -u origin <branch-name>`
- Create PR: `gh pr create --title "..." --body "..."`
- PR body should include: Summary, Changes, Testing done

### 7. Verify CI
- Wait for CI checks: `gh pr checks <pr-number>`
- If tests fail, fix locally and push again
- Key jobs to verify:
  - Lint Code
  - Build Extension
  - Test Extension (Mock LLM)
  - Test MCP External (if MCP changes)

### 8. Reflect, Review, Clean Up
- Self-review the PR diff: `gh pr diff <pr-number>`
- Check for:
  - Unused code or debug statements
  - Missing error handling
  - Documentation updates needed
  - Test coverage gaps
- Request user approval before merge
- After merge, verify master CI passes
- Delete the feature branch

## Quick Commands

```bash
# Create issue
gh issue create --title "title" --body "description"

# Create branch
git fetch origin && git switch -c issue-123-feature origin/master

# Run tests
npm run test && node tests/extension.mock.test.js

# Create PR
gh pr create --title "feat: description" --body "## Summary\n..."

# Check CI
gh pr checks <pr-number>

# Merge (after approval)
gh pr merge <pr-number> --squash --delete-branch
```

## When to Use the Torvalds Agent

**USE the `torvalds` agent for any of these tasks:**

- Implementing new features
- Fixing bugs
- Refactoring code
- Working on GitHub issues
- Any task requiring: code changes + commit + PR + merge

**Invoke with:** `@torvalds <task description>`

The torvalds agent follows a strict 17-step protocol:

| Phase | Step |
|-------|------|
| 1-3 | Think, Create Issue, Create Branch |
| 4-6 | Implement, Commit, Push |
| 7-9 | Create PR, Self-Review, Reflect |
| 10-12 | Wait PR-CI, Request Approval, Merge |
| 13-17 | Wait Master-CI, Verify Deploys, Health Check, Close Issue, Report |

See `.opencode/agents/torvalds.md` for full protocol.

# Github issues
If asked to work on github issue, **use the torvalds agent**: `@torvalds Work on GitHub issue #<number>`

The torvalds agent will:
1. Fetch issue / task using gh cli tool, git fetch, git switch -c issue-<number> origin/master;
2. Think, research, come with a plan, use TODO
3. If this is a new issue, try to reproduce by extending @tests/extension.mock.test.js or @tests/vibe-e2e.test.js (what fits better)
4. Create PR, review, reflect.
5. Check github action checks
6. Wait for user approval before merge
7. Merge and close the github issue
8. Verify master CI passes and deployments succeed

## Building a Production AI SaaS on Azure for Under $100/Month
**File:** `docs/systemDesign.md`
**Category:** services

# Building a Production AI SaaS on Azure for Under $100/Month

*How we architected Vibe Browser's backend infrastructure to serve thousands of users while keeping costs minimal*

---

## The Challenge

When building an AI-powered browser extension that needs to:
- Proxy requests to multiple LLM providers (OpenAI, Azure, Anthropic)
- Handle user subscriptions with tiered access (Free/Pro/Max)
- Serve documentation with AI-powered chat support
- Process payments via Stripe

...the typical approach would cost $500-1000+/month in cloud infrastructure. We built it for under $100/month.

---

## Architecture Overview

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                    VIBE BROWSER AZURE INFRASTRUCTURE                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─ Resource Group: vibe-ai-rg (Production AI) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                                                                      │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐                         │
│  │ vibe-openai-1766209650 (eastus) │    │ info-mjnxtt51-eastus2 (eastus2) │                         │
│  │ Azure OpenAI - S0               │    │ Azure AIServices                │                         │
│  │ ┌─────────────────────────────┐ │    │ ┌─────────────────────────────┐ │                         │
│  │ │ gpt-5-mini  (GlobalStd)     │ │    │ │ gpt-5.2     (GlobalStd)     │ │                         │
│  │ │ gpt-5       (GlobalStd)     │ │    │ │ gpt-oss-120b(GlobalStd)     │ │                         │
│  │ │ gpt-5-nano  (GlobalStd)     │ │    │ │ grok-4      (GlobalStd)     │ │                         │
│  │ │ gpt-5.1     (DataZoneStd)   │ │    │ │ grok-4-fast (GlobalStd)     │ │                         │
│  │ └─────────────────────────────┘ │    │ │ DeepSeek-R1 (GlobalStd)     │ │                         │
│  └─────────────────────────────────┘    │ └─────────────────────────────┘ │                         │
│                                          └─────────────────────────────────┘                         │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────┐                                                                 │

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                     External Services                                                │
│  ┌─────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────┐              │
│  │    Supabase     │   │     Stripe      │   │    PostgreSQL   │   │   Cloudflare    │              │
│  │  (Auth/Users)   │   │  (Payments)     │   │   (LiteLLM DB)  │   │   (DNS/SSL)     │              │
│  │    Free tier    │   │   2.9% + $0.30  │   │   Supabase DB   │   │   Free tier     │              │
│  └─────────────────┘   └─────────────────┘   └─────────────────┘   └─────────────────┘              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Cost Breakdown

### Monthly Infrastructure Costs

| Resource | Type | SKU | Monthly Cost | Notes |
|----------|------|-----|--------------|-------|
| **vibe-backend-vm** | Virtual Machine | Standard_B2ms | **$60.74** | 2 vCPU, 8GB RAM, runs all backend services |
| **vibeextensioncdn** | Storage Account | Standard_LRS | **~$1** | Extension ZIP hosting, minimal storage |
| **vibebrowser-docs** | Static Web App | Free | **$0** | Documentation site with serverless API |
| **vibe-portal** | Static Web App | Free | **$0** | OAuth authentication portal |
| **vibebrowser-docs-search** | AI Search | Free | **$0** | 50MB index, 3 indexes max |
| **Azure OpenAI** | Cognitive Services | S0 | **$0 base** | Pay-per-token only |
| **Azure AIServices** | Cognitive Services | S0 | **$0 base** | Pay-per-token only |
| **Communication Services** | Email | Pay-per-use | **~$0.25** | $0.00025/email for support escalation |

### External Services

| Service | Tier | Monthly Cost | Notes |
|---------|------|--------------|-------|
| **Supabase** | Free | **$0** | Auth, 50K monthly active users |
| **Cloudflare** | Free | **$0** | DNS, SSL, DDoS protection |
| **Stripe** | Pay-per-transaction | **2.9% + $0.30** | Only charged on actual payments |
| **Domain** | Annual | **~$1/month** | vibebrowser.app |

### Total Fixed Monthly Cost: **~$63**

The variable costs (AI tokens, emails, Stripe fees) scale with usage but start at $0.

---

## Key Architecture Decisions

### 1. Single VM with k3s Instead of Kubernetes Cluster

**Why not AKS (Azure Kubernetes Service)?**
- AKS minimum: ~$150/month (3-node cluster)
- Our traffic doesn't justify multi-node redundancy yet

**Our approach:**
- Single Standard_B2ms VM ($60/month)
- k3s (lightweight Kubernetes) for container orchestration
- Traefik for ingress routing
- All services run as Kubernetes deployments

```yaml
# Example: LiteLLM deployment
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: litellm
  namespace: vibe
spec:
  replicas: 1
  template:
    spec:
      containers:
        - name: litellm
          image: ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:main-stable
          resources:
            requests:
              memory: "512Mi"
              cpu: "250m"
            limits:
              memory: "1Gi"
              cpu: "1000m"
```

### 2. Azure Static Web Apps for Free Hosting

Static Web Apps provide:
- Free SSL certificates
- Global CDN
- Serverless Functions (for docs chat API)
- GitHub Actions integration

We use two Static Web Apps:
- `docs.vibebrowser.app` - Docusaurus documentation + chat widget
- `auth.vibebrowser.app` - OAuth callback handling

### 3. Pay-Per-Token AI Models (No PTU)

**Critical lesson learned:** Azure OpenAI offers two pricing models:

| Model | PTU (Provisioned) | Standard (Pay-per-token) |
|-------|-------------------|--------------------------|
| Billing | Fixed monthly (~$120/PTU) | Per 1K tokens |
| Cost if unused | **Full price** | $0 |
| Use case | High-volume production | Variable traffic |

We accidentally deployed a PTU reservation that cost **$12,000 in a few days** with zero usage. Always use `GlobalStandard` or `DataZoneStandard` for new projects:

```terraform
# CORRECT - Pay-per-token
scale {
  type     = "GlobalStandard"
  capacity = 100
}

# WRONG - Fixed monthly cost regardless of usage
scale {
  type     = "DataZoneProvisionedManaged"
  capacity = 100
}
```

### 4. Supabase for Authentication

Instead of building auth:
- Supabase Free tier: 50K monthly active users
- Built-in OAuth (Google, GitHub, etc.)
- JWT tokens for API authentication
- PostgreSQL database included (used by LiteLLM)

### 5. Azure AI Search Free Tier for RAG

The documentation chat uses Retrieval-Augmented Generation:
- Free tier: 50MB storage, 3 indexes
- Vector search + semantic ranking
- Sufficient for ~500 documentation pages

---

## Service Architecture on k3s

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    k3s Cluster (namespace: vibe)                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                 │
│  ┌─────────────┐  ┌─────────────┐  ┌─────────────┐             │
│  │   LiteLLM   │  │   Stripe    │  │    User     │             │
│  │   Proxy     │  │   Service   │  │   Portal    │             │
│  │             │  │             │  │             │             │
│  │ • API proxy │  │ • Webhooks  │  │ • OAuth     │             │
│  │ • Rate limit│  │ • Billing   │  │ • Key mgmt  │             │
│  │ • Usage log │  │ • Tiers     │  │             │             │
│  └──────┬──────┘  └──────┬──────┘  └──────┬──────┘             │
│         │                │                │                     │
│         └────────┬───────┴────────┬───────┘                     │
│                  │                │                             │
│           ┌──────▼──────┐  ┌──────▼──────┐                      │
│           │   Traefik   │  │   Secrets   │                      │
│           │   Ingress   │  │  ConfigMap  │                      │
│           └──────┬──────┘  └─────────────┘                      │
│                  │                                              │
└──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                   │
            ┌──────▼──────┐
            │  Public IP  │
            │  Port 80    │
            └─────────────┘
```

### LiteLLM (API Gateway)

Routes requests to appropriate AI providers based on model selection:

```yaml
model_list:
  # Free tier
  - model_name: gpt-5-mini
    litellm_params:
      model: azure/gpt-5-mini
      api_base: ${AZURE_OPENAI_BASE_URL}
  
  # Pro tier  
  - model_name: gpt-5.1
    litellm_params:
      model: azure/gpt-5-1
      
  # Max tier
  - model_name: gpt-5.2
    litellm_params:
      model: azure/gpt-5-2
      api_base: ${EASTUS2_OPENAI_BASE_URL}
```

### Stripe Service

Handles subscription lifecycle:
- Webhook processing for payment events
- Tier-based API key provisioning
- Usage budget management

### User Portal

- OAuth flow completion
- API key display
- Subscription management UI

---

## Request Flow

### API Request (Extension → LLM)

```
1. Extension sends request to api.vibebrowser.app/v1/chat/completions
2. Cloudflare terminates SSL, forwards to VM
3. Traefik routes /v1/* to LiteLLM service
4. LiteLLM validates JWT token (Supabase)
5. LiteLLM checks user tier and rate limits
6. LiteLLM proxies to appropriate Azure OpenAI endpoint
7. Response returned to extension
```

### Authentication Flow

```
1. User clicks "Sign In" in extension
2. Extension opens auth.vibebrowser.app
3. Static Web App redirects to Supabase OAuth
4. User authenticates with Google/GitHub
5. Supabase returns JWT token
6. Extension stores token, calls /stripe/register
7. Stripe service creates LiteLLM API key
8. Extension uses API key for future requests
```

---

## Scaling Considerations

### Current Capacity (Single VM)

- ~1000 concurrent API requests
- ~10,000 daily active users
- 8GB RAM shared across all services

### When to Scale

| Metric | Current | Scale Trigger |
|--------|---------|---------------|
| CPU usage | <50% | >80% sustained |
| Memory | <6GB | >7GB sustained |
| Response time | <200ms | >500ms p95 |

### Scaling Options

1. **Vertical**: Upgrade VM to Standard_B4ms ($120/month)
2. **Horizontal**: Add second VM + load balancer (~$150/month)
3. **Managed**: Migrate to AKS when >$200/month makes sense

---

## Lessons Learned

### 1. Always Use Pay-Per-Token for AI Models

PTU (Provisioned Throughput) sounds attractive but costs $120+/PTU/month regardless of usage. We wasted $12K learning this.

### 2. Free Tiers Are Production-Ready

Azure Static Web Apps, Supabase Auth, and Cloudflare Free tiers handle real production traffic without issues.

### 3. k3s > Docker Compose for Single-VM

k3s provides:
- Rolling deployments
- Health checks and auto-restart
- Secrets management
- Same YAML as production Kubernetes

### 4. Consolidate Services Aggressively

Running 4 separate small VMs costs more than 1 VM with all services. Our 2-vCPU VM comfortably runs:
- LiteLLM (API proxy)
- Stripe webhook handler
- User portal
- Traefik ingress
- PostgreSQL could run here too (we use Supabase's)

---

## Conclusion

Building a production AI SaaS doesn't require expensive infrastructure. By leveraging:
- Free tiers strategically (Static Web Apps, Supabase, Cloudflare)
- Pay-per-use AI models (no PTU reservations)
- Consolidated compute (single VM with k3s)
- Managed services for complexity (Stripe for payments, Supabase for auth)

We run a complete AI browser extension backend for **~$63/month** in fixed costs, with variable costs scaling only with actual usage.

The architecture supports growth to ~10,000 DAU before requiring any scaling decisions, at which point revenue should easily cover infrastructure upgrades.

---

*Have questions about this architecture? Visit [docs.vibebrowser.app](https://docs.vibebrowser.app) or reach out at support@vibebrowser.app*
