# Langfuse Azure Production Deployment

This Terraform configuration deploys Langfuse on Azure with production-ready infrastructure.

## Architecture

- **App Service**: Linux Web App for containers with auto-scaling and high availability
- **PostgreSQL Flexible Server**: Managed database with backups and geo-replication
- **Application Insights**: Monitoring, logging, and alerting
- **Key Vault**: Secure secret management
- **Custom Domain**: Optional SSL-enabled custom domain support

## Features

✅ **Production Ready**
- HTTPS/SSL termination
- Auto-scaling and load balancing
- Health checks and monitoring
- High availability

✅ **Security**
- Secrets stored in Key Vault
- Restricted database access
- HTTPS-only traffic
- Managed identities

✅ **Monitoring & Observability**
- Application Insights integration
- Health check endpoints
- Structured logging
- Performance monitoring

✅ **Backup & Recovery**
- PostgreSQL automated backups
- Configurable retention periods
- Point-in-time restore

## Quick Start

```bash
# Initialize Terraform
./deploy.sh init

# Plan deployment
./deploy.sh plan

# Deploy
./deploy.sh apply
```

## Configuration

### Variables

| Variable | Default | Description |
|----------|---------|-------------|
| `environment` | `dev` | Environment name |
| `location` | `centralus` | Azure region |
| `custom_domain` | `""` | Custom domain (optional) |
| `enable_monitoring` | `true` | Enable Application Insights |
| `enable_key_vault` | `true` | Enable Key Vault for secrets |
| `postgres_backup_retention_days` | `7` | Database backup retention |

### Custom Domain Setup

1. Add CNAME record pointing to your App Service hostname
2. Deploy with custom domain:

```bash
terraform apply -var='custom_domain=yourdomain.com'
```

3. Configure SSL certificate in Azure Portal

## Security Considerations

- Database firewall allows only Azure services
- Secrets are stored in Key Vault when enabled
- HTTPS is enforced
- Managed identities used for service authentication

## Monitoring

Access Application Insights in Azure Portal for:
- Performance metrics
- Error tracking
- Request telemetry
- Custom alerts

## Backup & Recovery

PostgreSQL backups are automatically configured with:
- Daily backups
- Configurable retention (default: 7 days)
- Geo-redundant storage
- Point-in-time restore capability

## Troubleshooting

### Common Issues

1. **Container startup failures**: Check App Service logs in Azure Portal
2. **Database connection issues**: Verify firewall rules and connection strings
3. **SSL certificate issues**: Ensure custom domain is properly configured
4. **Key Vault access**: Check managed identity permissions

### Logs

- **App Service logs**: Available in Azure Portal > App Service > Logs
- **Application Insights**: Azure Portal > Application Insights
- **Database logs**: Azure Portal > PostgreSQL server > Logs

## Cost Optimization

- Use Basic tier (B1) for development
- Scale to Standard/Premium for production
- Monitor usage and adjust SKU as needed
- Enable auto-scaling for variable workloads

## Migration from Container Instances

This configuration replaces the previous ACI-based deployment with production-ready App Service. Key improvements:

- **Reliability**: No single points of failure
- **Security**: HTTPS, Key Vault, restricted access
- **Monitoring**: Full observability stack
- **Scalability**: Auto-scaling capabilities
- **Maintenance**: Managed service updates</content>

## Model Access Control Architecture
**File:** `services/subscription/docs/model-access-control.md`
**Category:** services

# Model Access Control Architecture

## Overview

Vibe Browser implements tier-based model access using **user-level restrictions** in LiteLLM. This architecture ensures that subscription changes take effect immediately for all user API keys without requiring regeneration.

## Implementation (Jan 2026)

### Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      SUBSCRIPTION CHANGE FLOW                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. User upgrades from Free → Pro via Stripe Checkout
                    ↓
2. Stripe webhook fires: subscription.created / subscription.updated
                    ↓
3. stripe-service receives webhook
                    ↓
4. Identify tier from price_id (STRIPE_PRO_PRICE_ID)
                    ↓
5. Update LiteLLM user.models array via /user/update
   POST /user/update
   {
     "user_id": "xxx",
     "models": ["gpt-5-mini", "gpt-oss-120b", "gpt-4.1", 
                "gpt-5.1", "grok-4-fast", "deepseek-v3.2"],
     "max_budget": 25.0,
     "budget_duration": "30d",
     "metadata": {"tier": "pro"}
   }
                    ↓
6. All existing API keys automatically respect new model restrictions
                    ↓
7. User can immediately access Pro-tier models
```

### Key Concept: Inheritance vs. Key-Level Restrictions

**User-Level Restrictions (Current Implementation):**
- User has `models` array: `["gpt-5-mini", "gpt-oss-120b", "gpt-4.1"]`
- Keys created **without** `models` parameter inherit from user
- When user.models changes, ALL keys respect new restrictions instantly

**Key-Level Restrictions (Previous Approach - Deprecated):**
- Keys created **with** `models` parameter have fixed restrictions
- Key-level restrictions take precedence over user-level
- Subscription changes require key regeneration (bad UX)

## Code Implementation

### 1. Stripe Webhook Handler

**File:** `services/subscription/stripe-service/server.js`

**Lines 851-880:** `updateLiteLLMBudget()` function

```javascript
async function updateLiteLLMBudget(userId, tier) {
  const budget = BUDGETS[tier]; // { max_budget, budget_duration, models }
  
  const response = await fetch(`${LITELLM_URL}/user/update`, {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${LITELLM_MASTER_KEY}`,
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      user_id: userId,
      max_budget: budget.max_budget,
      budget_duration: budget.budget_duration,
      models: budget.models,              // ← CRITICAL: Set user-level models
      metadata: { tier },                 // ← Store tier for reference
    }),
  });
  
  return response.json();
}
```

**Key Points:**
- `models` array defines allowed models at user level
- `metadata.tier` stores tier for debugging/admin purposes
- No need to update individual keys

### 2. User Portal Key Generation

**File:** `services/subscription/user-portal/server.js`

**Lines 269-285:** `/api/auth/token` endpoint

```javascript
const keyResponse = await litellmRequest('POST', '/key/generate', {
  user_id: userId,
  key_alias: `vibe-${userId.slice(0, 8)}-${Date.now().toString(36)}`,
  max_budget: tierConfig.max_budget,
  budget_duration: tierConfig.budget_duration,
  duration: '365d',
  // models: omitted - inherits from user.models (set by stripe-service webhook)
  // tier: omitted - stored at user level only, not in key metadata
  metadata: { email }
});
```

**Key Points:**
- `models` parameter is **omitted** from key generation
- `tier` is **omitted** from key metadata (stored only at user level)
- Keys automatically inherit restrictions from user.models
- Subscription changes don't require key regeneration
- Tier displayed in admin dashboard comes from user.metadata.tier, not key

### 3. Tier Configuration

**File:** `services/subscription/stripe-service/server.js`

**Lines 36-40:** `BUDGETS` constant

```javascript
const BUDGETS = {
  free: { 
    max_budget: 1.0, 
    budget_duration: '24h', 
    models: ['gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-oss-120b', 'gpt-4.1'] 
  },
  pro: { 
    max_budget: 25.0, 
    budget_duration: '30d', 
    models: ['gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-oss-120b', 'gpt-4.1', 
             'gpt-5.1', 'grok-4-fast', 'deepseek-v3.2'] 
  },
  max: { 
    max_budget: 99.0, 
    budget_duration: '30d', 
    models: ['gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-oss-120b', 'gpt-4.1', 
             'gpt-5.1', 'grok-4-fast', 'deepseek-v3.2', 
             'gpt-5.2', 'grok-4', 'grok-4-fast-reasoning', 'deepseek-r1'] 
  },
};
```

**Model Names:**
- Model names are LiteLLM IDs (without `vibe:` prefix)
- LiteLLM config maps these to actual Azure deployments
- Example: `gpt-5-mini` → `azure/gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18`

## Testing

### Automated Tests

**File:** `services/subscription/tests/tier-models.test.ts`

Tests verify that:
1. Free users can only access 3 models
2. Pro users can access 6 models (free + 3 premium)
3. Max users can access 10 models (pro + 4 premium)
4. Model restrictions are properly enforced by LiteLLM

**Run tests:**
```bash
cd services/subscription
npm run test:tier
```

### Manual Verification

**1. Check user's model restrictions:**
```bash
curl "https://api.vibebrowser.app/user/info?user_id=USER_ID" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_MASTER_KEY" | \
  jq '.user_info | {models, metadata, max_budget}'
```

**2. Test model access with user's API key:**
```bash
# Should succeed for allowed models
curl "https://api.vibebrowser.app/v1/chat/completions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer USER_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "model": "gpt-5-mini",
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "test"}]
  }'

# Should fail with 403 for restricted models
curl "https://api.vibebrowser.app/v1/chat/completions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer USER_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "model": "gpt-5.2",
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "test"}]
  }'
```

## Debugging

### Common Issues

**Issue:** User upgraded but still can't access premium models

**Diagnosis:**
```bash
# 1. Check user's models array
kubectl exec -n vibe deployment/stripe-service -- node -e "
  fetch('http://litellm:4000/user/info?user_id=USER_ID', {
    headers: {'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + process.env.LITELLM_MASTER_KEY}
  }).then(r => r.json()).then(d => {
    console.log('User models:', d.user_info.models);
    console.log('User tier:', d.user_info.metadata.tier);
  });
"

# 2. Check if keys have key-level restrictions (shouldn't have)
# If keys[0].models exists, the key has key-level restrictions (bad)
# If keys[0].models is undefined/null, it inherits from user (good)
```

**Fix:**
```bash
# Update user.models manually if webhook failed
kubectl exec -n vibe deployment/stripe-service -- node -e "
  const BUDGETS = {
    pro: { models: ['gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-oss-120b', 'gpt-4.1', 
                    'gpt-5.1', 'grok-4-fast', 'deepseek-v3.2'] }
  };
  
  fetch('http://litellm:4000/user/update', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + process.env.LITELLM_MASTER_KEY,
      'Content-Type': 'application/json'
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      user_id: 'USER_ID',
      models: BUDGETS.pro.models,
      metadata: { tier: 'pro' }
    })
  }).then(r => r.json()).then(console.log);
"
```

## Migration Notes

### From Key-Level to User-Level Restrictions

**Before (Key-Level - Deprecated):**
- Keys created with `models` parameter
- Subscription changes required key regeneration
- Each key had independent restrictions

**After (User-Level - Current):**
- Keys created without `models` parameter
- Keys inherit from user.models
- Subscription changes update user.models
- All keys respect new tier instantly

**Migration:**
Existing keys with key-level restrictions continue to work, but won't respect tier changes. Users should regenerate keys via the user portal to get inheritance-based keys.

## Benefits

1. **Instant Tier Changes**: Subscription upgrades/downgrades take effect immediately
2. **No Key Regeneration**: Users don't need to update keys in their applications
3. **Centralized Control**: Single source of truth for model access (user.models)
4. **Simpler Logic**: Fewer edge cases to handle in webhook processing
5. **Better UX**: Seamless experience when changing subscriptions

## Related Files

- `services/subscription/stripe-service/server.js` - Webhook handler, budget logic
- `services/subscription/user-portal/server.js` - Key generation
- `services/subscription/tests/tier-models.test.ts` - Tier access tests
- `services/subscription/litellm-config.yaml` - Model definitions
- `services/subscription/docs/tiers.md` - Tier documentation
- `.github/workflows/deploy-subscription.yml` - CI/CD with tier tests

- Stripe Webhooks: https://stripe.com/docs/webhooks

# Subscription Services

LiteLLM API gateway with Stripe billing for Vibe Browser.

## Components

```bash
# Get cluster access
./services/k3s/setup-k3s-access.ts
export KUBECONFIG=~/.kube/vibe.config

# Deploy
./services/subscription/deploy.ts deploy
./services/subscription/deploy.ts status
```

## Model Access Control

User-level restrictions in LiteLLM:
- `user.models` array defines allowed models
- API keys inherit from user (no key-level restrictions)
- Stripe webhooks update user.models on tier change
- No key regeneration needed

See `@docs/tiers.md` for tier details.

## Updating Tiers

1. Edit `stripe-service/server.js` BUDGETS constant
2. Edit `scripts/manage.ts` BUDGETS constant (keep in sync)
3. Deploy: `gh workflow run deploy-subscription.yml -f environment=prod`
4. Test: `npm run test:tier`

## Stripe Webhook Setup

The Stripe webhook receives subscription events at `https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook`.

### Install Stripe CLI

```bash
brew install stripe/stripe-cli/stripe
```

### List Webhooks

```bash
# Get API key from k8s secret
STRIPE_KEY=$(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d)

# List current webhooks
stripe webhook_endpoints list --live --api-key $STRIPE_KEY
```

### Create Webhook (Production)

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints create \
  --live \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY \
  -d url="https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.created" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.updated" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.deleted" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.paid" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.payment_failed" \
  -d description="VibeBrowser Production Subscription Webhooks"
```

### Create Webhook (Dev)

```bash
STRIPE_KEY_DEV=$(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe-dev -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d)

stripe webhook_endpoints create \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY_DEV \
  -d url="https://api-dev.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.created" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.updated" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.deleted" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.paid" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.payment_failed" \
  -d description="VibeBrowser Dev Subscription Webhooks"
```

### Update Webhook URL

```bash
# Get endpoint ID from list command, then update
stripe webhook_endpoints update WEBHOOK_ENDPOINT_ID \
  --live \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY \
  -d url="https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook"
```

### Delete Webhook

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints delete WEBHOOK_ENDPOINT_ID --live --confirm --api-key $STRIPE_KEY
```

### Verify Webhook Works

```bash
# Should return "No stripe-signature header" error (expected)
curl -X POST https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{}'
```

## Google OAuth Configuration

# Google OAuth Configuration

This document describes the Google OAuth setup for Vibe Browser extension testing.

## Google Cloud Project

| Property | Value |
|----------|-------|
| Project ID | `vibebrowser-481803` |
| Project Name | VibeBrowser |
| Owner Account | `vibeteaichnologies@gmail.com` |
| Console URL | https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials?authuser=2&project=vibebrowser-481803 |

## OAuth 2.0 Client

| Property | Value |
|----------|-------|
| Client Name | Vibe Browser Extension OAuth |
| Client ID | `349491536786-p5ljprl35rqhfpc0446cp9nsdti1gojh.apps.googleusercontent.com` |
| Client Secret | GitHub secret `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_CLIENT_SECRET` |
| Type | Web application |

### Authorized Redirect URIs

| URI | Purpose |
|-----|---------|
| `https://ajfjlohdpfgngdjfafhhcnpmijbbdgln.chromiumapp.org/` | Chrome extension OAuth flow |
| `http://localhost:8080` | Local testing with setup script |

### Authorized JavaScript Origins

| Origin | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `chrome-extension://ajfjlohdpfgngdjfafhhcnpmijbbdgln` | Extension origin |

## Scopes

The OAuth client requests the following scopes:

| Scope | Purpose |
|-------|---------|
| `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.readonly` | Read Gmail messages |
| `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.send` | Send emails |
| `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.events` | Manage calendar events |
| `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.readonly` | Read calendar |
| `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.readonly` | Read Google Drive files |

## Testing Setup

### One-Time Setup (Manual OAuth Consent)

Run the setup script to obtain a refresh token:

```bash
# Get secret from GitHub: gh secret list | grep GOOGLE_WORKSPACE
# Or from local .env file (source .env first)
GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_CLIENT_SECRET='<secret>' bun scripts/setup-google-test-account.ts
```

This will:
1. Start a local server on port 8080
2. Open a browser to Google OAuth consent page
3. After you grant access, save tokens to `.secrets/google-oauth-test.json`

The refresh token is **long-lived** (6+ months) and survives browser restarts. You only need to run this once per Google account.

### Running Tests

Once the refresh token is saved, tests can run automatically:

```bash
bun tests/google-workspace.test.js
```

The test will:
1. Load the refresh token from `.secrets/google-oauth-test.json`
2. Exchange it for a fresh access token via Google OAuth API
3. Inject tokens into extension's `chrome.storage.local` via CDP
4. Run Gmail/Calendar API tests

### Token Storage

| File | Contents | Git-ignored |
|------|----------|-------------|
| `.secrets/google-oauth-test.json` | OAuth refresh token for testing | Yes |

### CI/CD Setup

For CI, the refresh token should be stored as a GitHub secret:

```bash
# Store as secret
gh secret set GOOGLE_REFRESH_TOKEN < .secrets/google-oauth-test.json
```

The CI workflow would then:
1. Restore the secret to `.secrets/google-oauth-test.json`
2. Run the test as normal

## Token Injection Architecture

```
+-------------------+      +------------------+      +-------------------+
|  .secrets/        |      |   Test Runner    |      |  Extension        |
|  google-oauth-    | ---> |  (Puppeteer)     | ---> |  chrome.storage   |
|  test.json        |      |                  |      |  .local           |
+-------------------+      +------------------+      +-------------------+
        |                          |                         |
        |                          |                         v
        |                    CDP Session              GoogleWorkspaceTools.js
        |                          |                         |
        v                          v                         v
   refresh_token          Runtime.evaluate            Gmail/Calendar API
        |                   injects tokens                   |
        v                          |                         v
   Google OAuth API               |                    Real API calls
   (token refresh)                |
```

Key insight: **Refresh tokens are NOT tied to browser sessions**. They are credentials that can be exchanged for access tokens via a simple HTTPS POST request.

## Troubleshooting

### "redirect_uri_mismatch" Error

The redirect URI `http://localhost:8080` must be registered in Google Cloud Console:
1. Go to https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials?authuser=2&project=vibebrowser-481803
2. Click on "Vibe Browser Extension OAuth"
3. Under "Authorized redirect URIs", ensure `http://localhost:8080` is listed
4. Save

### "Token refresh failed" Error

The refresh token may have been revoked or expired. Re-run the setup script:

```bash
# Get secret from GitHub: gh secret list | grep GOOGLE_WORKSPACE
# Or from local .env file (source .env first)
GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_CLIENT_SECRET='<secret>' bun scripts/setup-google-test-account.ts
```

### Permission Denied

If you need to modify OAuth settings, you must use the `vibeteaichnologies@gmail.com` account which owns the project.

## Legacy Notes

Other OAuth clients in the project:
- `349491536786-d1qr92eunqbfgstikin5jjb40ijg4qpb` - Has redirect URI for Supabase callback

## Release Process
**File:** `docs/release.md`
**Category:** deployment

# Release Process

## Quick Release

```bash
# 1. Create and push tag
git tag v1.0.0
git push origin v1.0.0

# 2. Create GitHub release
gh release create v1.0.0 --title "v1.0.0" --notes "Release notes"
```

CI automatically:
- Builds extension with version from tag
- Creates ZIP package
- Uploads to Azure CDN (latest + versioned)
- Attaches artifacts to GitHub release

## Release Artifacts

| Artifact | Location | Purpose |
|----------|----------|---------|
| Latest ZIP | `vibeextensioncdn/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip` | Public download |
| Versioned ZIP | `vibeextensioncdn/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-v{ver}-{commit}.zip` | Version history |
| GitHub Release | `github.com/VibeTechnologies/VibeWebAgent/releases` | Internal distribution |

## Production Build (Manual)

```bash
# Full production build
./scripts/deploy-production.sh

# Build only (no publish)
./scripts/deploy-production.sh --no-publish

# Build + publish to Chrome Web Store
./scripts/deploy-production.sh --publish
```

Output:
- `dist/VibeBrowserExtension-v{VERSION}-webstore.zip` - Chrome Web Store
- `dist/VibeBrowserExtension-v{VERSION}.crx` - Direct install

## Chrome Web Store Publishing

1. Build: `npm run build:extension:prod && npm run zip`
2. Go to [Chrome Developer Dashboard](https://chrome.google.com/webstore/devconsole)
3. Upload `dist/VibeBrowserExtension-v*.zip`
4. Fill metadata (see `docs/chromeWebStore.md`)
5. Submit for review

## Azure CDN Distribution

Primary public download URL:
```
https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip
```

Manual upload (if CI fails):
```bash
az storage blob upload \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip \
  --file ./dist/VibeBrowserExtension-v*.zip \
  --overwrite \
  --auth-mode key \
  --account-key "$AZURE_STORAGE_KEY"
```

## Version Bump

Version is set in:
- `package.json`
- `manifest.json`

CI updates these automatically from git tag on release.

## Pre-Release E2E Verification

Before any release, perform full end-to-end production verification:

### 1. Download Extension from CDN

```bash
# Download latest extension from production CDN
curl -L -o /tmp/vibe-extension.zip \
  https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip

# Verify download succeeded and file is valid
unzip -t /tmp/vibe-extension.zip
```

### 2. Install Extension in Browser

1. Open Chrome/Brave and navigate to `chrome://extensions`
2. Enable "Developer mode" (toggle in top right)
3. Drag and drop `/tmp/vibe-extension.zip` onto the page
4. Verify extension installs without errors

### 3. Test Production API Connection

1. Open extension sidepanel (click extension icon or press shortcut)
2. Configure with production API (api.vibebrowser.app)
3. Send a simple query: "What is 2+2?"
4. Verify response is received from LLM

### 4. Test Core Features

| Feature | Test | Expected Result |
|---------|------|-----------------|
| Navigation | "Go to google.com" | Browser navigates to Google |
| Click | "Click the search button" | Element is clicked |
| Fill | "Type hello in search box" | Text is entered |
| Vision | Enable vision toggle, send query | Screenshot included in request |
| Model Switch | Change model in dropdown | New model used for next query |

### 5. Test Subscription Tiers

```bash
# Verify tier endpoints respond
curl -s https://api.vibebrowser.app/api/subscription/{user-id} | jq .tier
```

### 6. Automated E2E Test

```bash
# Run production E2E test (uses real API)
VIBE_API_URL=https://api.vibebrowser.app npm run test:e2e:prod
```

### Release Checklist

- [ ] All workflows passing (CI/CD, Deploy Subscription, Docs Deploy)
- [ ] Extension downloads from CDN successfully
- [ ] Extension installs without errors
- [ ] Production API responds (api.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Dev API responds (api-dev.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Stripe webhooks respond (400 = healthy, missing signature)
- [ ] Portal accessible (portal.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Docs accessible (docs.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Basic agent query works end-to-end
- [ ] No new Sentry errors in last 24h

## Rollback

```bash
# Download previous version
az storage blob download \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-v{old-version}.zip \
  --file ./rollback.zip

# Upload as latest
az storage blob upload \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip \
  --file ./rollback.zip \
  --overwrite \
  --account-key "$AZURE_STORAGE_KEY"
```

## Langfuse Deployment
**File:** `services/langfuse/README.md`
**Category:** deployment

# Langfuse Deployment

LLM observability platform on Azure App Service.

**URL:** https://langfuse.vibebrowser.app

## Quick Start

```bash
cd services/langfuse
./deploy.sh init   # Initialize Terraform
./deploy.sh plan   # Preview changes
./deploy.sh apply  # Deploy
```

## Architecture

```
                    +-----------------+
                    |   Cloudflare    |
                    |  (DNS + SSL)    |
                    +--------+--------+
                             |
                    +--------v--------+
                    | Azure App       |
                    | Service (B1)    |
                    | langfuse:2      |
                    +--------+--------+
                             |
              +--------------+--------------+
              |              |              |
     +--------v------+ +-----v-----+ +------v------+
     | PostgreSQL    | | Key Vault | | App Insights|
     | Flexible B1ms | | (secrets) | | (monitoring)|
     +---------------+ +-----------+ +-------------+
```

## Resources Created

| Resource | SKU | Purpose |
|----------|-----|---------|
| App Service Plan | B1 | Hosts Langfuse container |
| Linux Web App | - | Langfuse v2 Docker image |
| PostgreSQL Flexible Server | B_Standard_B1ms | Database |
| Key Vault | Standard | Secrets storage |
| Application Insights | - | Monitoring |

## Configuration

| Variable | Default | Description |
|----------|---------|-------------|
| `environment` | `dev` | Environment name |
| `location` | `centralus` | Azure region |
| `custom_domain` | `""` | Custom domain (e.g., langfuse.vibebrowser.app) |
| `langfuse_version` | `2` | Docker image tag (use v2, v3 requires Clickhouse) |
| `postgres_backup_retention_days` | `7` | Backup retention |

## Deployment Commands

```bash
# Full deployment with custom domain
./deploy.sh apply
terraform apply -var="custom_domain=langfuse.vibebrowser.app"

# View outputs
./deploy.sh output

# Destroy all resources
./deploy.sh destroy
```

## Account Setup

Langfuse uses self-registration. No default credentials exist.

### First-Time Setup

1. Go to https://langfuse.vibebrowser.app/auth/sign-up
2. Create an account with your email and password
3. Create a new project
4. Go to **Settings > API Keys** to get your keys

### Get API Keys

After creating a project, retrieve keys for integration:

```bash
# Add to your .env or extension config
LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
LANGFUSE_BASE_URL=https://langfuse.vibebrowser.app
```

### Team Access

To add team members:
1. Go to **Settings > Members**
2. Invite by email
3. New members sign up at `/auth/sign-up` and accept invitation

## Post-Deployment: Fix ENCRYPTION_KEY

Langfuse v2 requires ENCRYPTION_KEY in 64-character hex format (not base64). After initial Terraform deployment, run:

```bash
# Generate proper hex key
ENCRYPTION_KEY=$(openssl rand -hex 32)

# Update App Service setting
az webapp config appsettings set \
  --name $(terraform output -raw app_service_name) \
  --resource-group $(terraform output -raw resource_group_name) \
  --settings ENCRYPTION_KEY="$ENCRYPTION_KEY"

# Restart to apply
az webapp restart \
  --name $(terraform output -raw app_service_name) \
  --resource-group $(terraform output -raw resource_group_name)
```

## Custom Domain Setup

1. Create CNAME record in Cloudflare:
   ```
   langfuse.vibebrowser.app -> <app-service-name>.azurewebsites.net
   ```

2. Deploy with custom domain:
   ```bash
   terraform apply -var="custom_domain=langfuse.vibebrowser.app"
   ```

3. SSL is handled by Cloudflare (Full mode).

## Troubleshooting

### Check Application Logs

```bash
# Download logs
az webapp log download \
  --name $(terraform output -raw app_service_name) \
  --resource-group $(terraform output -raw resource_group_name) \
  --log-file /tmp/langfuse-logs.zip

# Stream live logs
az webapp log tail \
  --name $(terraform output -raw app_service_name) \
  --resource-group $(terraform output -raw resource_group_name)
```

### Common Issues

| Issue | Cause | Fix |
|-------|-------|-----|
| 500 error on startup | Invalid ENCRYPTION_KEY format | Run hex key fix above |
| Container crash loop | Key Vault reference timeout | Set secrets as plain values via `az webapp config` |
| Database connection refused | Firewall rule missing | Check Azure services firewall rule exists |

### Restart App Service

```bash
az webapp restart \
  --name $(terraform output -raw app_service_name) \
  --resource-group $(terraform output -raw resource_group_name)
```

## Cost

~$50-80/month (Basic tier for dev)

| Resource | Monthly Cost |
|----------|-------------|
| App Service B1 | ~$13 |
| PostgreSQL B1ms | ~$25 |
| Key Vault | ~$1 |
| Application Insights | Usage-based |

## Version Notes

- **Langfuse v2**: Only requires PostgreSQL. Simple setup.
- **Langfuse v3**: Requires PostgreSQL + Clickhouse + Redis + S3. Use v2 unless you need v3 features.

## Stripe Service
**File:** `services/subscription/stripe-service/README.md`
**Category:** services

# Stripe Service

Handles Stripe webhooks and subscription management for Vibe Browser.

## Endpoint

| Environment | URL |
|-------------|-----|
| Production | `https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook` |
| Dev | `https://api-dev.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook` |

## Webhook Setup via CLI

### Install Stripe CLI

```bash
brew install stripe/stripe-cli/stripe
```

### Get API Key

```bash
# Production
STRIPE_KEY=$(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d)

# Dev
STRIPE_KEY_DEV=$(kubectl get secret vibe-secrets -n vibe-dev -o jsonpath='{.data.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY}' | base64 -d)
```

### List Webhooks

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints list --live --api-key $STRIPE_KEY
```

### Create Webhook (Production)

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints create \
  --live \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY \
  -d url="https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.created" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.updated" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.deleted" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.paid" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.payment_failed" \
  -d description="VibeBrowser Production Subscription Webhooks"
```

### Create Webhook (Dev)

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints create \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY_DEV \
  -d url="https://api-dev.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.created" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.updated" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=customer.subscription.deleted" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.paid" \
  -d "enabled_events[]=invoice.payment_failed" \
  -d description="VibeBrowser Dev Subscription Webhooks"
```

### Update Webhook URL

```bash
# Get endpoint ID from list command, then update
stripe webhook_endpoints update WEBHOOK_ENDPOINT_ID \
  --live \
  --api-key $STRIPE_KEY \
  -d url="https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook"
```

### Delete Webhook

```bash
stripe webhook_endpoints delete WEBHOOK_ENDPOINT_ID --live --confirm --api-key $STRIPE_KEY
```

### Verify Webhook Works

```bash
# Should return "No stripe-signature header" error (expected - TLS works)
curl -X POST https://api.vibebrowser.app/stripe/webhook -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{}'
```

## Troubleshooting

### TLS Errors

If Stripe reports TLS errors, the webhook URL is likely using wrong subdomain.

**Valid hostnames with SSL certificates:**
- `api.vibebrowser.app`
- `api-dev.vibebrowser.app`

**Invalid (no SSL cert):**
- `stripe.api.vibebrowser.app` (wildcard DNS resolves but no cert)

Fix by updating the webhook URL via CLI (see above).

## Handled Events

| Event | Action |
|-------|--------|
| `customer.subscription.created` | Provision user tier in LiteLLM |
| `customer.subscription.updated` | Update user tier/budget |
| `customer.subscription.deleted` | Downgrade to free tier |
| `invoice.paid` | Reset monthly budget |
| `invoice.payment_failed` | Log warning |

# CI/CD Pipeline

```
              +------------------+
```

## Workflows

| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `ci-cd.yml` | Push, PR, release | Main pipeline: build, test, deploy |
| `deploy-subscription.yml` | Manual | Backend services manual deployment |
| `docs-deploy.yml` | Push to master | Documentation site deployment |

## ci-cd.yml Jobs by Stage

### Stage 1: Lint & Build (all triggers)
- **lint**: Run ESLint
- **detect-changes**: Check if `services/subscription/**` changed
- **build-dev-extension**: Build extension with dev config
- **build-npm**: Build NPM package

### Stage 2: Mock Test (all triggers)
- **test-mock**: Run `tests/extension.mock.test.js` with mock LLM server

### Stage 3: Deploy Dev Services (if services changed)
- **build-service-images**: Build Docker images for stripe-service, user-portal
- **deploy-dev-services**: Deploy to `vibe-dev` K8s namespace

### Stage 4: E2E Test Dev (all triggers)
- **e2e-test-dev**: Run `tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --env=dev`

### Stage 5: Build Prod Extension (master only)
- **build-prod-extension**: Build extension with prod config

### Stage 6: Deploy Prod Services (master + services changed)
- **deploy-prod-services**: Deploy to `vibe` K8s namespace

### Stage 7: E2E Test Prod (master only)
- **e2e-test-prod**: Run `tests/vibe-e2e.test.js --env=prod`
- **test:tier**: Verify tier access on prod API

### Stage 8: Deploy CDN (master only)
- **deploy-cdn**: Upload extension to Azure CDN

## Artifacts

| Artifact | Job | Contents |
|----------|-----|----------|
| `extension-dev` | build-dev-extension | Dev extension build |
| `extension-prod` | build-prod-extension | Prod extension build |
| `extension-prod-zip` | build-prod-extension | Webstore-ready ZIP |
| `npm-package` | build-npm | NPM tarball |
| `mock-test-screenshots` | test-mock | Screenshots from mock test |
| `e2e-dev-results` | e2e-test-dev | Dev E2E test artifacts |
| `e2e-prod-results` | e2e-test-prod | Prod E2E test artifacts |

## Required Secrets

| Secret | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `AZURE_STORAGE_KEY` | CDN upload to `vibeextensioncdn` |
| `K3S_KUBECONFIG` | Kubernetes cluster access |
| `EXTENSION_PUBLIC_KEY` | Extension signing |
| `SENTRY_DSN` | Error tracking |
| `LANGFUSE_*` | Telemetry |
| `OPENAI_API_KEY` | LiteLLM backend |
| `STRIPE_*` | Payment processing |
| `E2E_TEST_ACCOUNTS` | E2E test credentials |

## Conditional Logic

Services deployment jobs only run when `services/subscription/**` files change:
- Uses `dorny/paths-filter@v3` to detect changes
- E2E tests run regardless (test against existing deployed APIs)

## Environments

| Environment | K8s Namespace | API URL |
|-------------|---------------|---------|
| dev | vibe-dev | api-dev.vibebrowser.app |
| prod | vibe | api.vibebrowser.app |

## Screenshot Upload to PR

After test completion:
```bash
./scripts/create-gh-report.sh
```

This uploads screenshots as PR comments for visual verification.

## Extension Distribution
**File:** `docs/distribution.md`
**Category:** deployment

# Extension Distribution

This document describes how Vibe AI Copilot extension packages (.zip) are built, distributed, and installed.

## Distribution Channels

### 1. Azure CDN (Public Downloads)

The primary distribution channel for public downloads. Files are automatically uploaded on every `master` branch push and release.

| File | URL |
|------|-----|
| Latest ZIP | https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip |

Versioned files follow the naming convention:
- `vibe-ai-copilot-v{version}-{commit}.zip`

### 2. GitHub Releases (Internal)

GitHub releases contain the same artifacts but require authentication since the repository is private. Used for internal distribution and version tracking.

Release URL: https://github.com/VibeTechnologies/VibeWebAgent/releases

## Build Artifacts

| Artifact | Description | Use Case |
|----------|-------------|----------|
| `.zip` | Unpacked extension archive | Developer mode installation, Chrome Web Store submission |

## CI/CD Pipeline

The distribution is handled by `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml` in the `build-extension` job.

### Trigger Conditions

Azure CDN upload occurs when:
- Push to `master` branch
- GitHub release is created

### Build Process

1. **Version Update** (releases only): Updates `package.json` and `manifest.json` with release tag version
2. **Production Build**: Runs `npm run build:extension:prod`
3. **ZIP Creation**: Runs `npm run zip` to create distribution package
4. **Azure Upload**: Runs `npm run distribute` to upload to Azure Blob Storage
5. **GitHub Release**: Attaches artifacts to the release (if applicable)

### Required Secrets

| Secret | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `AZURE_STORAGE_KEY` | Azure Storage account key for `vibeextensioncdn` |
| `SENTRY_DSN` | Sentry error tracking DSN |
| `LANGFUSE_*` | Langfuse telemetry keys |

## Azure Storage Configuration

- **Storage Account**: `vibeextensioncdn`
- **Resource Group**: `vibebrowser`
- **Container**: `extensions`
- **Access Level**: Public blob access (anonymous read)
- **Region**: East US

### Manual Upload (if needed)

```bash
# Upload a file manually
az storage blob upload \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip \
  --file ./dist/VibeBrowserExtension-v*.zip \
  --overwrite \
  --auth-mode key \
  --account-key "$AZURE_STORAGE_KEY"
```

## Installation Instructions

### Chrome / Chromium / Brave - ZIP Drag and Drop (Recommended)

1. Download the ZIP file from Azure CDN: [Download ZIP](https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip)
2. Open browser and navigate to [chrome://extensions](chrome://extensions)
3. Enable "Developer mode" (toggle in top right)
4. Drag and drop the ZIP file onto the extensions page
5. Click "Add extension" when prompted

### Alternative: Load Unpacked

1. Download and extract the ZIP file to a folder
2. Open browser and navigate to [chrome://extensions](chrome://extensions)
3. Enable "Developer mode" (toggle in top right)
4. Click "Load unpacked"
5. Select the extracted folder

## Product Page Integration

The product page at https://www.vibebrowser.app uses Azure CDN URLs for download buttons:

```tsx
// Download button points to:
href="https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip"
```

## Creating a New Release

1. **Create and push a version tag**:
   ```bash
   git tag v0.0.3
   git push origin v0.0.3
   ```

2. **Create GitHub Release**:
   ```bash
   gh release create v0.0.3 --title "v0.0.3" --notes "Release notes here"
   ```

3. **CI/CD automatically**:
   - Builds the extension with the new version
   - Creates ZIP package
   - Uploads to Azure CDN (both versioned and latest)
   - Attaches artifacts to the GitHub release

## Troubleshooting

### Azure Upload Fails

1. Verify `AZURE_STORAGE_KEY` secret is set correctly
2. Check storage account firewall settings
3. Ensure container `extensions` exists with public access

### ZIP Not Generated

1. Check build logs for errors
2. Ensure `npm run build:extension:prod` completed successfully
3. Verify `npm run zip` script exists

### Version Mismatch

If the CDN has old versions:
1. Check the latest CI run on `master` branch
2. Verify the build completed successfully
3. Manually trigger a release if needed

## File Structure

```
dist/
├── extension/                    # Unpacked extension (for testing)
└── VibeBrowserExtension-v*.zip   # Web Store package

# After distribution:
vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip        # Latest ZIP for CDN
vibe-ai-copilot-v*-*.zip          # Versioned ZIP
```

## Release Process
**File:** `.opencode/skills/release/SKILL.md`
**Category:** deployment

---
name: release
description: Create and publish VibeBrowser extension releases. Includes tagging, CDN upload, E2E verification, and rollback procedures.
---

# Release Process

## Quick Release

```bash
# 1. Create and push tag
git tag v1.0.0
git push origin v1.0.0

# 2. Create GitHub release
gh release create v1.0.0 --title "v1.0.0" --notes "Release notes"
```

CI automatically:
- Builds extension with version from tag
- Creates ZIP package
- Uploads to Azure CDN (latest + versioned)
- Attaches artifacts to GitHub release

## Release Artifacts

| Artifact | Location | Purpose |
|----------|----------|---------|
| Latest ZIP | `vibeextensioncdn/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip` | Public download |
| Versioned ZIP | `vibeextensioncdn/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-v{ver}-{commit}.zip` | Version history |
| GitHub Release | `github.com/VibeTechnologies/VibeWebAgent/releases` | Internal distribution |

## Pre-Release E2E Verification

Before any release, perform full end-to-end production verification:

### 1. Download Extension from CDN

```bash
curl -L -o /tmp/vibe-extension.zip \
  https://vibeextensioncdn.blob.core.windows.net/extensions/vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip

unzip -t /tmp/vibe-extension.zip
```

### 2. Install Extension in Browser

1. Open Chrome/Brave and navigate to `chrome://extensions`
2. Enable "Developer mode" (toggle in top right)
3. Drag and drop `/tmp/vibe-extension.zip` onto the page
4. Verify extension installs without errors

### 3. Test Production API Connection

1. Open extension sidepanel (click extension icon or press shortcut)
2. Configure with production API (api.vibebrowser.app)
3. Send a simple query: "What is 2+2?"
4. Verify response is received from LLM

### 4. Test Core Features

| Feature | Test | Expected Result |
|---------|------|-----------------|
| Navigation | "Go to google.com" | Browser navigates to Google |
| Click | "Click the search button" | Element is clicked |
| Fill | "Type hello in search box" | Text is entered |
| Vision | Enable vision toggle, send query | Screenshot included in request |
| Model Switch | Change model in dropdown | New model used for next query |

### 5. Test Subscription Tiers

```bash
curl -s https://api.vibebrowser.app/api/subscription/{user-id} | jq .tier
```

### 6. Automated E2E Test

```bash
VIBE_API_URL=https://api.vibebrowser.app npm run test:e2e:prod
```

## Release Checklist

- [ ] All workflows passing (CI/CD, Deploy Subscription, Docs Deploy)
- [ ] Extension downloads from CDN successfully
- [ ] Extension installs without errors
- [ ] Production API responds (api.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Dev API responds (api-dev.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Stripe webhooks respond (400 = healthy, missing signature)
- [ ] Portal accessible (portal.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Docs accessible (docs.vibebrowser.app)
- [ ] Basic agent query works end-to-end
- [ ] No new Sentry errors in last 24h

## Production Build (Manual)

```bash
# Full production build
./scripts/deploy-production.sh

# Build only (no publish)
./scripts/deploy-production.sh --no-publish

# Build + publish to Chrome Web Store
./scripts/deploy-production.sh --publish
```

## Rollback

```bash
# Download previous version
az storage blob download \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-v{old-version}.zip \
  --file ./rollback.zip

# Upload as latest
az storage blob upload \
  --account-name vibeextensioncdn \
  --container-name extensions \
  --name vibe-ai-copilot-latest.zip \
  --file ./rollback.zip \
  --overwrite \
  --account-key "$AZURE_STORAGE_KEY"
```

## Building an AI-Powered Documentation Portal with Automated Support on Azure
**File:** `services/docusarus/README.md`
**Category:** deployment

# Building an AI-Powered Documentation Portal with Automated Support on Azure

*How we built a self-service documentation portal that deflects 80% of support tickets while offering seamless human escalation.*

This document details the architecture and implementation of the Vibe Browser documentation portal (`docs.vibebrowser.app`), which combines Docusaurus, Azure OpenAI, and Azure Communication Services to provide an intelligent support experience.

## 1. System Architecture

The system is built entirely on serverless Azure infrastructure, keeping costs low while scaling automatically.

```mermaid
graph TD
    User[User] -->|HTTPS| CDN[Azure Static Web App]
    CDN -->|/api/chat| FuncChat[Azure Function: Chat]
    CDN -->|/api/escalate| FuncEscalate[Azure Function: Escalate]
    
    subgraph "Knowledge Retrieval"
        FuncChat -->|Search| Search[Azure AI Search]
        Search -->|Context| FuncChat
    end
    
    subgraph "Reasoning"
        FuncChat -->|Context + Query| OpenAI[Azure OpenAI (gpt-5-mini)]
        OpenAI -->|Response| FuncChat
    end
    
    subgraph "Escalation"
        FuncEscalate -->|Send Email| ACS[Azure Communication Services]
        ACS -->|SMTP| SupportEmail[support@vibebrowser.app]
        SupportEmail -->|Routing| Gmail[Support Inbox]
    end
```

## 2. Request Flow

When a user interacts with the documentation chat:

1. **Ingestion**: User asks "How do I configure custom providers?"
2. **Retrieval**: System searches `docs-index` in Azure AI Search for top 5 relevant chunks.
3. **Generation**: 
    - Retrieved chunks + User Query + System Prompt are sent to `gpt-5-mini`.
    - Model generates a response with citations and clickable `<suggestion>` tags.
4. **Interaction**: User gets an answer. If unsatisfied, they click "Talk to a Human".
5. **Escalation**:
    - Full chat transcript is captured.
    - `/api/escalate` triggers an email via Azure Communication Services.
    - Ticket #ID is generated and returned to user.

## 3. Documentation Storage

We use [Docusaurus](https://docusaurus.io/) as the content engine. Documentation lives in markdown files with frontmatter metadata.

**Directory Structure:**
```
services/docusarus/
├── docs/                          # Shared Documentation Source
│   ├── intro.md
│   ├── using-copilot.md
│   └── getting-started/
│       └── configuration.md
├── docusaurus-azure-chat/         # Frontend App
│   ├── docusaurus.config.ts
│   └── src/
└── deploy.sh                      # Unified Deployment Script
```

**Frontmatter Example:**
```markdown
---
title: Configuring Providers
sidebar_position: 2
slug: /providers
description: How to set up BYOK for OpenAI, Anthropic, and Gemini.
---

# Configuring Providers
...
```

## 4. Deployment & Tracking

We use a unified `deploy.sh` script that handles the entire pipeline. This script is idempotent and can be run locally or in CI/CD.

### The Pipeline (`deploy.sh`)

1. **Infrastructure**: Provision/Verify Azure Resources (SWA, OpenAI, Search, ACS) using Azure CLI.
2. **Schema**: Update Azure AI Search index schema if needed.
3. **Build**: `npm run build` generates the static Docusaurus site.
4. **Deploy**: Upload static assets and API functions to Azure Static Web Apps.
5. **Index**: Run `scripts/index-to-azure-search.ts` to parse markdown, generate embeddings, and update the search index.

### CI/CD Integration

GitHub Actions triggers deployment only when documentation files change, saving build minutes.

```yaml
on:
  push:
    paths:
      - 'services/docusarus/**'
```

## 5. Human Escalation

The "Talk to a Human" feature bridges the gap between AI and human support.

**Mechanism:**
1. The Chat UI parses `<suggestion>Talk to a Human</suggestion>` tags from the AI response.
2. When clicked, a modal asks for the user's email.
3. `api/escalate` is called with `{ email, transcript, pageUrl }`.
4. **Azure Communication Services** sends a formatted HTML email to `support@vibebrowser.app`.
5. Cloudflare Email Routing forwards this to our actual support inbox (Gmail).
6. The email Reply-To is set to the user's email, so support agents can reply directly.

**Infrastructure:**
Managed via Terraform (`terraform/main.tf`) to configure:
- Communication Service resource
- Custom domain verification (SPF, DKIM, DMARC) via Cloudflare DNS records.

## 6. The "Brain": System Prompt

The intelligence comes from a carefully crafted system prompt that instructs the model on tone, constraints, and formatting.

**Current System Prompt (excerpt):**

```text
You are a helpful documentation assistant for Vibe Browser. Answer questions based on the documentation provided below. Be concise and friendly.

IMPORTANT GUIDELINES:
1. If you find relevant information in the docs, provide a clear answer with specific steps when applicable.
2. If the question is about account management, billing, or technical issues NOT covered in the docs, respond helpfully and suggest escalation.
3. If the user seems frustrated, confused, or explicitly asks to speak with someone, proactively suggest the "Talk to a Human" option.

SUGGESTION FORMAT:
At the end of EVERY response, include 1-3 clickable suggestions wrapped in <suggestion> tags.
- <suggestion>Talk to a Human</suggestion> - for support escalation
- <suggestion>How do I install?</suggestion> - follow-up
```

## 7. Azure Stack Integration

We chose the Azure stack for seamless integration and enterprise-grade reliability:

| Component | Role | Benefit |
|-----------|------|---------|
| **Azure Static Web Apps** | Hosting + API | Zero-config SSL, global CDN, integrated Functions. |
| **Azure OpenAI** | Intelligence | Private networking, compliance, `gpt-5-mini` speed. |
| **Azure AI Search** | Memory | Vector + Semantic search hybrid for best retrieval. |
| **Azure Communication Services** | Email | High deliverability, programmable email API. |

*Maintained by the Vibe Engineering Team*

## K3s Terraform Infrastructure
**File:** `services/k3s/terraform/README.md`
**Category:** deployment

# K3s Terraform Infrastructure

Terraform configuration for managing K3s worker nodes.

## Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    vibe-backend-rg2                         │
│                                                             │
│  ┌─────────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────────┐        │
│  │  vibe-backend-vm    │    │   vibe-worker-01    │        │
│  │  (master/control)   │◄──►│   (worker)          │        │
│  │  Standard_B2ms      │    │   Standard_B2s      │        │
│  │  10.0.0.4           │    │   10.0.0.5          │        │
│  └─────────────────────┘    └─────────────────────┘        │
│              │                         │                    │
│              └─────────┬───────────────┘                    │
│                        │                                    │
│            vibe-backend-vmVNET (10.0.0.0/16)               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Prerequisites

1. Azure CLI authenticated: `az login`
2. Terraform installed: `brew install terraform`
3. SSH key at `~/.ssh/id_rsa.pub`

## Usage

```bash
cd services/k3s/terraform

# Initialize
terraform init

# Preview
terraform plan

# Apply
terraform apply
```

## Adding More Workers

Edit `variables.tf`:
```hcl
variable "worker_count" {
  default = 2  # Change from 1 to 2
}
```

Then apply:
```bash
terraform apply
```

## Variables

| Variable | Default | Description |
|----------|---------|-------------|
| `worker_count` | 1 | Number of worker nodes |
| `worker_vm_size` | Standard_B2s | VM size (~$30/month) |
| `location` | westus2 | Azure region |
| `master_private_ip` | 10.0.0.4 | Master node IP |

## State

State is stored in Azure Storage:
- Resource Group: `vibe-terraform-state`
- Storage Account: `vibetfstate`
- Container: `tfstate`
- Key: `k3s.tfstate`

## Secrets

K3s node token is stored in Azure Key Vault:
- Key Vault: `vibe-k3s-kv`
- Secret: `k3s-node-token`

To update the token:
```bash
# SSH to master
ssh azureuser@4.246.58.241
sudo cat /var/lib/rancher/k3s/server/node-token

# Update Key Vault
az keyvault secret set --vault-name vibe-k3s-kv \
  --name k3s-node-token \
  --value "<token>"
```

## Destroying Workers

```bash
terraform destroy
```

This only removes worker nodes. Master is not managed by Terraform.

## Troubleshooting

Check cloud-init on worker:
```bash
ssh -J azureuser@4.246.58.241 azureuser@10.0.0.5
sudo cloud-init status
sudo journalctl -u k3s-agent
```

Check node status:
```bash
kubectl get nodes
kubectl describe node vibe-worker-01
```

## CLAUDE.md - @presto-ai/google-workspace-mcp
**File:** `services/mcp/CLAUDE.md`
**Category:** deployment

# CLAUDE.md - @presto-ai/google-workspace-mcp

## Project Overview

**This is a minimal npm distribution wrapper** for the upstream [gemini-cli-extensions/workspace](https://github.com/gemini-cli-extensions/workspace) Google Workspace MCP server.

**Purpose**: Enable easy installation via `npx @presto-ai/google-workspace-mcp` for employees to access Google Workspace services (Gmail, Calendar, Drive, Docs, Sheets, Chat) from Claude Desktop/Code.

**Philosophy**: This project is a glorified shim and CI/CD pipeline. We do NOT maintain custom code - we simply package upstream for npm distribution.

## What This Project Does

1. **Syncs source from upstream** - All code in `src/` comes directly from `gemini-cli-extensions/workspace`
2. **Customizes paths.ts only** - Changes token storage to `~/.config/google-workspace-mcp/` instead of project root
3. **Publishes to npm** - Makes it installable via `npx`

## What This Project Does NOT Do

- No custom tests (upstream handles testing)
- No custom linting infrastructure
- No custom features or modifications
- No divergence from upstream behavior

## Essential Commands

```bash
npm install          # Install dependencies
npm run build        # Build TypeScript → dist/
npm link             # Test locally
google-workspace-mcp --auth  # Authenticate
```

## Project Structure

```
google-workspace-mcp/
├── .github/workflows/
│   ├── publish.yml           # Publishes to npm on GitHub release
│   └── sync-and-publish.yml  # Daily sync from upstream
├── bin/
│   └── cli.js                # Entry point (shebang script)
├── src/                      # SYNCED FROM upstream (do not modify)
│   └── utils/
│       └── paths.ts          # ONLY file we customize
├── dist/                     # Built output (gitignored)
├── esbuild.config.js         # From upstream
├── package.json              # Our npm config
└── README.md
```

## The One Customization: paths.ts

We override `src/utils/paths.ts` to store tokens in a persistent user config directory:

- **macOS/Linux**: `~/.config/google-workspace-mcp/`
- **Windows**: `%APPDATA%/google-workspace-mcp/`
- **Override**: `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_MCP_HOME` environment variable

This is necessary because `npx` runs from ephemeral cache directories.

## CI/CD Workflows

### 1. Upstream Sync (`.github/workflows/sync-and-publish.yml`)

- **Runs**: Daily at 6 AM UTC + manual trigger
- **Does**:
  1. Checks for new commits in upstream
  2. Copies source files from `upstream/main:workspace-server/src/`
  3. Applies our custom `paths.ts`
  4. Bumps patch version
  5. Creates GitHub release (triggers publish)

### 2. npm Publish (`.github/workflows/publish.yml`)

- **Runs**: On GitHub release creation
- **Does**: `npm ci && npm run build && npm publish`
- **Auth**: Uses OIDC trusted publishing (no npm tokens stored)

## Upstream Sync Details

The sync workflow tracks the last synced commit in `.last-upstream-sync`. When upstream has new commits:

1. Deletes current `src/` directory
2. Copies fresh files via `git show upstream/main:workspace-server/src/...`
3. Writes our custom `paths.ts` (hardcoded in workflow)
4. Commits with version bump
5. Creates release to trigger npm publish

## Local Development

```bash
# Clone and setup
git clone https://github.com/jrenaldi79/google-workspace-mcp.git
cd google-workspace-mcp
npm install

# Build and test
npm run build
npm link
google-workspace-mcp --auth   # Opens browser for OAuth
google-workspace-mcp          # Runs MCP server

# Cleanup
npm unlink -g @presto-ai/google-workspace-mcp
```

## Manual Upstream Sync

If you need to sync manually:

```bash
git fetch upstream
git show upstream/main:workspace-server/src/index.ts > src/index.ts
# ... (repeat for other files)
# Keep our custom paths.ts
npm run build
npm link
# Test, then commit and push
```

## Publishing

Publishing happens automatically when:
1. Sync workflow detects upstream changes → creates release
2. Release creation triggers publish workflow

Manual publish (rare):
```bash
npm run build
npm publish --access public
```

## Response Constraints for Claude

When working on this project:

- **DO NOT add tests** - upstream handles testing
- **DO NOT add linting** - keep it minimal
- **DO NOT modify src/ files** - they're synced from upstream
- **ONLY modify paths.ts** if token storage behavior needs to change
- **Keep CI/CD simple** - just sync and publish

## Maintenance

This file should be updated when:
- [ ] Changing the sync workflow
- [ ] Changing the publish workflow
- [ ] Changing the paths.ts customization
- [ ] Adding new workflows

```
  "user_id": "uuid",
```

## LiteLLM Admin Endpoints

### Generate API Key
```
POST https://api.vibebrowser.app/key/generate

  "user_id": "uuid",
  "max_budget": 1.0,
  "duration": "24h"
}
```

### Get Key Info
```

```

### List Keys
```
GET https://api.vibebrowser.app/key/list
Authorization: Bearer <litellm_master_key>
```

## Authentication

- **User API Key**: `sk-...` format virtual key from LiteLLM
- **Master Key**: Admin key for LiteLLM management endpoints
- **Supabase JWT**: Used only during OAuth flow, then exchanged for LiteLLM key

## Vibe Browser Subscription Tiers
**File:** `services/subscription/docs/tiers.md`
**Category:** services

# Vibe Browser Subscription Tiers

## Tier Overview

| Tier | Price | Budget | Reset | Models Available |
|------|-------|--------|-------|------------------|
| Free | $0/month | $1 | Daily | gpt-5-mini, gpt-oss-120b, gpt-4.1 |
| Pro | $25/month | $25 | Monthly | + gpt-5.1, grok-4-fast, deepseek-v3.2 |
| Max | $99/month | $99 | Monthly | + gpt-5.2, grok-4, grok-4-fast-reasoning, deepseek-r1 |

**Note:** Budget equals the subscription price. Users get exactly what they pay for.

## Model Access Control

**Implementation:** User-level model restrictions (as of Jan 2026)

Model access is controlled at the **user level** in LiteLLM, not at the key level:

- Each user has a `models` array that defines allowed models
- API keys created for the user inherit these restrictions automatically
- When subscription tier changes, the webhook updates `user.models`
- All existing keys immediately respect the new tier without regeneration

**Key Benefits:**
- Subscription upgrades/downgrades take effect instantly
- No need to regenerate API keys when tier changes
- Centralized access control (single source of truth)
- Simpler key management (keys don't carry model restrictions)

**Implementation Details:**
- Stripe webhook handler updates `user.models` in LiteLLM
- User portal key generation omits `models` parameter (inherits from user)
- See `services/subscription/stripe-service/server.js` lines 851-880

## Model Pricing

All pricing is per 1 million tokens. Source: `litellm-config.yaml`

| Model | Input | Output | Tier | Azure Backend |
|-------|-------|--------|------|---------------|
| gpt-5-mini | $0.15 | $0.60 | Free | Azure OpenAI |
| gpt-oss-120b | $0.55 | $2.19 | Free | Azure AI Services (MAI-DS-R1) |
| gpt-5.1 | $2.50 | $10.00 | Pro | Azure OpenAI |
| grok-4-fast | $3.00 | $15.00 | Pro | Azure AI Services |
| deepseek-v3.2 | $0.27 | $1.10 | Pro | Azure AI Services |
| gpt-5.2 | $2.50 | $10.00 | Max | Azure AI Services |
| grok-4 | $3.00 | $15.00 | Max | Azure AI Services |
| grok-4-fast-reasoning | $3.00 | $15.00 | Max | Azure AI Services |
| deepseek-r1 | $0.55 | $2.19 | Max | Azure AI Services |

## Configuration Files

- **Pricing & Models**: `services/subscription/litellm-config.yaml`
- **Tier Logic & Budgets**: `services/subscription/stripe-service/server.js` (BUDGETS, TIER_MODELS constants)

## Updating Pricing

1. Edit `litellm-config.yaml` with new `input_cost_per_token` and `output_cost_per_token`
2. Deploy: `scp litellm-config.yaml azureuser@4.246.58.241:/opt/vibe-backend/`
3. Restart: `ssh azureuser@4.246.58.241 "cd /opt/vibe-backend && docker-compose restart litellm"`
4. Verify: Check `/model/info` endpoint for updated pricing

## Verification Commands

```bash
# Check model pricing
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/model/info" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_MASTER_KEY" | \
  jq '.data[] | {model_name, input: (.model_info.input_cost_per_token * 1000000), output: (.model_info.output_cost_per_token * 1000000)}'

# Check spend tracking
curl -s "https://api.vibebrowser.app/spend/logs?limit=5" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_MASTER_KEY" | \
  jq '.[] | {model, total_tokens, spend}'
```

## Notes

- Pricing is configured in LiteLLM config, not Stripe
- Without explicit pricing in config, LiteLLM defaults to $0 (no spend tracking)
- Azure pricing pages use dynamic loading ($- placeholders), verify from Azure portal billing

## OpenRouter OAuth Integration
**File:** `docs/openrouter-oauth.md`
**Category:** security

# OpenRouter OAuth Integration

## Overview

OpenRouter is now fully integrated as a provider in Vibe Browser with OAuth authentication support. Unlike OpenAI (which doesn't offer public OAuth), OpenRouter provides a simple OAuth PKCE flow that **actually works**.

## Why OpenRouter OAuth Works

| Feature | OpenAI | OpenRouter |
|---------|--------|------------|
| **Public OAuth** | ❌ No | Yes |
| **Client ID Required** | N/A | ❌ No |
| **Pre-registration** | N/A | ❌ No |
| **Works Now** | ❌ | |

## How It Works

### User Flow

1. User selects an OpenRouter model (e.g., `openrouter:anthropic/claude-3.5-sonnet`)
2. If no API key is set, a "Quick Connect" button appears
3. User clicks "Connect"
4. Redirected to OpenRouter login page
5. User authorizes Vibe Browser
6. Returns with permanent API key
7. API key stored securely in Chrome storage

### Technical Flow

```
┌─────────────┐
│   User      │
└──────┬──────┘
       │ 1. Select openrouter:* model
       ▼
┌─────────────────┐
│  Settings Page  │ (OAuth button appears)
└──────┬──────────┘
       │ 2. Click "Connect"
       ▼
┌─────────────────┐
│  OAuth Service  │ Generate PKCE challenge
└──────┬──────────┘
       │ 3. Redirect to OpenRouter
       ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│  https://openrouter.ai/auth?         │
│    callback_url=chrome-extension://  │
│    code_challenge=xxx                │
│    code_challenge_method=S256        │
│    state=random-uuid                 │
└──────┬───────────────────────────────┘
       │ 4. User logs in & approves
       ▼
┌─────────────────┐
│  Callback       │ Returns with code
└──────┬──────────┘
       │ 5. Exchange code for API key
       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  POST /api/v1/auth/keys             │
│  { code, code_verifier }            │
└──────┬──────────────────────────────┘
       │ 6. Returns: { key: "sk-or-v1-xxx" }
       ▼
┌─────────────────┐
│  Token Manager  │ Store API key
└─────────────────┘
```

## Implementation Details

### OAuth Configuration

```typescript
// apps/chat4/src/services/oauth.ts
openrouter: {
  clientId: 'NOT_REQUIRED',
  authUrl: 'https://openrouter.ai/auth',
  tokenUrl: 'https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/auth/keys',
  scopes: [],
  usesPKCE: true
}
```

### Authorization Request

```
GET https://openrouter.ai/auth?
  callback_url=https://<EXTENSION_ID>.chromiumapp.org/
  &code_challenge=<BASE64_SHA256_HASH>
  &code_challenge_method=S256
  &state=<RANDOM_UUID>
```

**Key Differences from Standard OAuth:**
- Uses `callback_url` instead of `redirect_uri`
- No `client_id` parameter
- No `response_type` parameter
- No `scope` parameter

### Token Exchange

```
POST https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/auth/keys
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "code": "auth_code_from_callback",
  "code_verifier": "random_string_from_pkce"
}
```

**Response:**
```json
{
  "key": "sk-or-v1-xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx"
}
```

**Key Differences:**
- Returns actual API key (not access_token)
- No refresh_token (keys don't expire)
- No expires_in (keys are permanent)
- Uses JSON POST (not form-urlencoded)

### Provider Configuration

**Already configured in:**
- `apps/chat4/src/constants/providers.ts` - Provider info
- `lib/utils/initChatModel.js` - Uses ChatOpenAI with custom baseURL
- `apps/chat4/src/constants/models.ts` - Popular OpenRouter models

**Base URL:**
```
https://openrouter.ai/api/v1
```

**OpenRouter is OpenAI-compatible**, so it uses the same `ChatOpenAI` class from LangChain.

## Available Models

OpenRouter provides access to models from multiple providers:

```typescript
// Anthropic via OpenRouter
'openrouter:anthropic/claude-3.5-sonnet'
'openrouter:anthropic/claude-3-opus'
'openrouter:anthropic/claude-3-haiku'

// Google via OpenRouter
'openrouter:google/gemini-2.5-flash-preview-09-2025'
'openrouter:google/gemini-2.0-flash-exp:free'

// Meta via OpenRouter
'openrouter:meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct'
'openrouter:meta-llama/llama-3.1-405b-instruct'

// DeepSeek via OpenRouter
'openrouter:deepseek/deepseek-chat'
'openrouter:deepseek/deepseek-r1'

// xAI via OpenRouter
'openrouter:x-ai/grok-4-fast'
'openrouter:x-ai/grok-2'

// And many more...
```

## User Benefits

### With OAuth:
- One-click authentication
- No copy/paste of API keys
- Automatic account linking
- User controls spending in OpenRouter dashboard
- Can revoke access anytime

### Without OAuth (Manual API Key):
- Still works fine
- User gets key from: https://openrouter.ai/keys
- Paste into settings
- Both methods supported

## Testing

### 1. Build Extension
```bash
npm run build:extension
```

### 2. Test OAuth Flow

1. Load extension in Chrome
2. Go to settings
3. Select model: `openrouter:anthropic/claude-3.5-sonnet`
4. "Quick Connect" button should appear
5. Click "Connect"
6. Should redirect to OpenRouter login
7. Log in with OpenRouter account
8. Approve access
9. Should return to Vibe with API key set

### 3. Verify API Key

Check in Chrome DevTools:
```javascript
chrome.storage.local.get('vibe_oauth_token_openrouter', (result) => {
  console.log(result);
});
```

Should show:
```json
{
  "vibe_oauth_token_openrouter": "{\"access_token\":\"sk-or-v1-xxx\",\"provider\":\"openrouter\",\"created_at\":1234567890}"
}
```

### 4. Test API Call

Try running a query with the OpenRouter model. Should work without manual API key.

## Troubleshooting

### OAuth button doesn't appear
- Ensure you selected an `openrouter:*` model
- Ensure no API key is already set
- Check browser console for errors

### Redirect fails
- Verify extension ID matches callback URL
- Check Chrome Identity API is available
- Look for errors in service worker console

### Token exchange fails
- Check network tab for API response
- Verify PKCE challenge/verifier match
- Ensure code hasn't expired (use quickly)

### API calls fail with OAuth key
- Verify key starts with `sk-or-v1-`
- Check OpenRouter dashboard for usage/limits
- Try manual API key to isolate OAuth vs API issue

## Security

### PKCE Protection
- Code verifier: 32 random bytes, base64url encoded
- Code challenge: SHA-256 hash of verifier
- Prevents authorization code interception attacks

### Storage
- API key stored in `chrome.storage.local`
- Chrome provides encryption at rest
- Only accessible by extension

### No Client Secret
- OpenRouter doesn't use client secrets (public client)
- PKCE replaces client secret for security
- Safe for browser extensions

## Comparison with Manual API Keys

| Aspect | OAuth | Manual API Key |
|--------|-------|----------------|
| **Setup** | One click | Copy/paste |
| **Security** | PKCE protected | User responsibility |
| **Revocation** | Via OpenRouter dashboard | Delete key |
| **User Experience** | Best | Good |
| **Reliability** | Same API key format | Same API key format |
| **Expiration** | Never | Never |

## Future Enhancements

- [ ] Show account info (email, credits) after OAuth
- [ ] Allow switching between multiple OpenRouter accounts
- [ ] Show usage statistics from OpenRouter API
- [ ] Add OAuth for other providers if they support it
- [ ] Implement token revocation on disconnect

## References

- [OpenRouter OAuth PKCE Docs](https://openrouter.ai/docs/use-cases/oauth-pkce)
- [OpenRouter API Reference](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/authentication)
- [Chrome Identity API](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/reference/identity/)
- [RFC 7636 - PKCE](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7636)

## Note: capacity=100 gives 100 requests/min and 100K tokens/min
**File:** `docs/azure.md`
**Category:** deployment

```shell
brew install azure-cli
az login

az cognitiveservices account list-models --name gpt-5-1-eval --resource-group vibebrowser -o table

az cognitiveservices account deployment create \
    --name gpt-5-1-eval \
    --resource-group vibebrowser \
    --deployment-name gpt-5-mini \
    --model-name gpt-5-mini \
    --model-version "2025-08-07" \
    --model-format OpenAI \
    --sku-capacity 100 \
    --sku-name GlobalStandard

# Note: capacity=100 gives 100 requests/min and 100K tokens/min
# Lower capacity (e.g., 1) will cause rate limit errors and request queueing
```

## Vibe AI Services

# Vibe AI Services

Azure deployment scripts for Vibe Browser backend.

## Directory Structure

```
services/
├── subscription/  # LiteLLM proxy, Stripe billing, user portal
├── docusarus/     # Documentation site with AI chat
├── langfuse/      # LLM observability
└── k3s/           # Kubernetes cluster management
```

## Quick Commands

```bash
# Subscription services (k3s)
./services/subscription/deploy.ts deploy
./services/subscription/deploy.ts status

# Documentation site
cd services/docusarus && ./deploy.sh

# Langfuse
cd services/langfuse && ./deploy.sh apply
```

## Manual Deployment

When GitHub Actions budget is exhausted, deploy manually:

```bash
# Deploy subscription services to dev
gh workflow run deploy-subscription.yml -f environment=dev

# Deploy subscription services to prod
gh workflow run deploy-subscription.yml -f environment=prod

# Deploy documentation site
gh workflow run docs-deploy.yml
```

To increase GitHub Actions budget, organization owners can adjust spending limits at:
`https://github.com/organizations/VibeTechnologies/settings/billing/spending_limit`

## Architecture

See `@services/design.md` for infrastructure diagrams and URL reference.

## Service Details

| Service | Documentation | URL |
|---------|--------------|-----|
| Subscription | `@services/subscription/README.md` | api.vibebrowser.app |
| Documentation | `@services/docusarus/README.md` | docs.vibebrowser.app |
| Langfuse | `@services/langfuse/README.md` | langfuse.vibebrowser.app |
| k3s Cluster | `@services/k3s/README.md` | 4.246.58.241 |

## Documentation Update Guide
**File:** `services/docusarus/AGENTS.md`
**Category:** deployment

# Documentation Update Guide

## Directory Structure

```
services/docusarus/
├── docs/                          # Documentation source files (Markdown)
│   ├── intro.md                   # Homepage (slug: /)
│   ├── using-copilot.md
│   ├── providers.md
│   ├── settings.md
│   ├── troubleshooting.md
│   └── getting-started/
│       ├── extension.md
│       └── configuration.md
├── docusaurus-azure-chat/         # Docusaurus project
│   ├── docusaurus.config.ts       # Site config (reads from ../docs)
│   ├── sidebars.ts                # Sidebar navigation
│   └── build/                     # Generated static site
└── deploy.sh                      # Deployment script
```

## Adding New Documentation Pages

1. **Create the markdown file** in `docs/` with frontmatter:
   ```markdown
   ---
   title: Page Title
   sidebar_position: 5
   ---

   # Page Title

   Content here...
   ```

2. **Add to sidebar** in `docusaurus-azure-chat/sidebars.ts`:
   ```typescript
   const sidebars: SidebarsConfig = {
     tutorialSidebar: [
       'intro',
       'new-page',  // Add your new page here
       // ...
     ],
   };
   ```

3. **Update cross-references** in `intro.md` or other pages if needed.

## Deployment

```bash
cd services/docusarus
./deploy.sh
```

The script:
- Builds Docusaurus static site
- Deploys to Azure Static Web Apps
- Updates search index

## Common Tasks

### Update existing page
Edit the `.md` file in `docs/`, commit, push, run `./deploy.sh`

### Add new top-level page
1. Create `docs/new-page.md`
2. Add `'new-page'` to `sidebars.ts`
3. Commit, push, run `./deploy.sh`

### Add page to Getting Started section
1. Create `docs/getting-started/new-page.md`
2. Add `'getting-started/new-page'` to the `items` array in `sidebars.ts`
3. Commit, push, run `./deploy.sh`

## Frontmatter Options

| Field | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| `title` | Page title (shown in sidebar and browser tab) |
| `sidebar_position` | Order in sidebar (lower = higher) |
| `slug` | Custom URL path (e.g., `slug: /` for homepage) |

## Local Preview

```bash
cd docusaurus-azure-chat
npm install
npm run start
```

Opens at http://localhost:3000

## Testing Protocol

After deployment, verify documentation using Chrome DevTools MCP:

```bash
# 1. Deploy
./deploy.sh

# 2. Verify with MCP tools
```

### MCP Verification Steps

1. **Open the docs site:**
   ```
   mcp__chrome-devtools__new_page url="https://yellow-forest-0f1c0f40f.6.azurestaticapps.net"
   ```

2. **Take snapshot and verify sidebar:**
   ```
   mcp__chrome-devtools__take_snapshot
   ```

   Check that sidebar contains all expected pages:
   - Welcome
   - Getting Started (expandable)
   - Using the Co-Pilot
   - Providers
   - Settings
   - Troubleshooting

3. **Click each new page and verify content loads:**
   ```
   mcp__chrome-devtools__click uid="<link_uid>"
   ```

4. **Verify key content is present:**
   - Homepage: Subscription table with Free/Pro/Max tiers
   - Providers: BYOK setup, supported providers table
   - Settings: All debugging options documented
   - Using the Co-Pilot: Agent/Ask modes, browser tools

5. **Close test page:**
   ```
   mcp__chrome-devtools__close_page pageIdx=1
   ```

### Checklist

- [ ] Deploy completed without errors
- [ ] All sidebar links present
- [ ] Each page loads without 404
- [ ] Tables render correctly
- [ ] Code blocks display properly
- [ ] Navigation between pages works

## Vibeapioauth
**File:** `docs/vibeApiOauth.md`
**Category:** security

                                                                                          
  Flow:                                                                                     
  1. User clicks Connect button                                                             
  2. Extension opens portal.vibebrowser.app/auth via chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow()    
  3. User authenticates (Google or email magic link)                                        
  4. Portal generates auth code and redirects back to extension                             
  5. Extension exchanges code + code_verifier for API key                                   
  6. Connection complete - popup closes automatically                                       
                                                                                            
  Files Changed:                                                                            
                                                                                            
  | File                         | Change                                                |  
  |------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|  
  | oauth.ts                     | Added Vibe config with PKCE (like OpenRouter)         |  
  | vibeAuth.ts                  | Simplified to ~130 lines, uses OAuth flow             |  
  | user-portal/server.js        | Added /api/auth/code and /api/auth/token endpoints    |  
  | user-portal/public/auth.html | Auth page handles callback_url, code_challenge, state |  
  | SettingsPage.tsx             | Default model vibe:gpt-5-mini, clean error handling   |  
                                                                                            
  Architecture:                                                                             
  Extension                    Portal                      Backend                          
      |                           |                           |                             
      |--launchWebAuthFlow------->|                           |                             
      |  (callback_url, challenge)|                           |                             
      |                           |--Supabase Auth----------->|                             
      |                           |<--Session-----------------|                             
      |                           |--POST /api/auth/code----->|                             
      |                           |<--{ code }----------------|                             
      |<--redirect?code=xxx-------|                           |                             
      |--POST /api/auth/token---->|-------------------------->|                             
      |  (code, code_verifier)    |                           |                             
      |<--{ key: "sk-..." }-------|<--------------------------|                             
      |                           |                           |                             
                                                                                            
  YO
